# Claude skin study: progress

One item per run, in this order. Aesthetics are interleaved so none waits long. Pages: `prototypes/skins/claude/<skin>.html` and `combined.html`, with the index at `prototypes/skins/claude/index.html`.

- [x] **wire** · Join: jump ring (Hinge). Brass ring swings through eye loops on each end and twists shut; click the ring to open
  - Built in `wire.html`, which is a new page: copper wire frames, coiled cabochon displays and bead buttons. Loops warm when near, a ghost ring shows when ready, the ring threads and twists shut, and clicking it opens it again. A "plain tuck" baseline shade is included so the shade state works.
- [x] **felt** · Join: zipper (Interlock). Teeth lift on approach, the pull runs the seam; the pull is the release grip
  - Built in `felt.html`, a new page: filter-puffed felt cushions, cotton patches, cross-stitch digits. The teeth stand up when near and light when ready. The cushions lean into a V and the slider climbs it in 12 fps stop-motion. Drag the felt pull down (or click it, or press Enter) to unzip. A "plain deflate" baseline shade is included. Not yet checked in a browser.
- [x] **gothic** · Join: half-rose window (Interlock). Halves rotate into alignment; the full rose is the indicator; turn it to release
  - Built in `gothic.html`, a new page: limestone facades with pinnacles, cresting, half-gables at the joining ends and lit quarry-glass windows. The tracery turns into line on approach, the light blooms out from the boss and spills colour onto the stone, and you turn the rose 60° (or click it, or press Enter) to release. A "plain lower" baseline shade is included. Not yet checked in a browser (browser access was blocked this run).
- [x] **hollow** · Join: silk stitch (Bind). A needle darts red thread through eyelets and pulls taut; slash to release
  - Built in `hollow.html`, a new page: ink-outlined carapace shells with scalloped bellies, iron scrolls with lanterns (lit while running), brass-eyelet straps and pinned parchment displays. The eyelets glint when near, and on ready a needle appears with a ghost of the lacing. The needle darts two passes into a double cross, then pulls taut with a bounce and knots off. A slash across the seam (or a click, or Enter) cuts it with a white flash. A "plain draw-in" baseline shade is included. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **elven** · Join: vine (Grow). Tendrils reach, spiral round each other and leaf out; drag apart to unwind
  - Built in `elven.html`, a new page: leaf-shaped night-glass casings with silver rims, tip filigree, a crescent crest and moonstone panes. Fiddlehead buds uncurl when near and their tips touch when ready. Tendrils grow into an over-and-under braid that curls round the opposite knops, leaves open along it, and a moonflower blooms. To release, pull a casing away (the braid strains, then lets go and unwinds) or click the flower. A "plain narrow" baseline shade is included. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Layer stack: felt cathedral (gothic structure, felt material, stop-motion)
  - Built in `combined.html`, a new page with a "Mix" switcher and a layer table per mix. These are stuffed felt kneelers with a gothic silhouette: half-gables, felt pinnacles with French-knot crockets, scalloped cresting, quilted ashlar stitching, appliqué glass borders and dark frieze nameplates. The join is the half-rose done in felt. Felt can't glow, so instead of lighting up, the rose has its petals sewn on clockwise in stop-motion after a squash that knocks out some fluff. Tacking stitches mark the ready state, and you turn the rose to unpick it. A "plain lower" baseline shade is included. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · Shade: bead strand (Distil). Widget collapses into a necklace; stones are statuses, a flat tag bead carries the name
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Bead strand" shade option. The plate fades, the copper frame squeezes flat into the strand wire, and the beads slide in from both clasps with a click: a slab bead for the name, round stones for statuses (amber running, labradorite paused, carnelian needs you, malachite passed), and a coiled cabochon for the key value. Eye loops stay, so shaded pendants still link by jump ring. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Shade: pocket (Sink). Widget slides into a felt pocket; a woven name tag and a cross-stitch value peek out
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Pocket" shade option. A patch pocket in contrasting felt (denim, raspberry, moss) pops up over the cushion's foot, with a blanket-stitched mouth, a running-stitch seam and bar tacks. The cushion drops in with a squash, the pocket rises to the nameplate line, and a woven name tag pops out. The key value is cross-stitched in cream under the mouth. All of it runs at 12 fps with boiling fibres, and zipped pairs stay zipped. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · Shade: clerestory (Distil). A row of lit lancets, one per status, with tooltips
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Clerestory" shade option. The wall lowers to one storey under the carved frieze, which keeps the name and key value. Lancets rise out of the string course bay by bay, then kindle left to right with a small flare: amber running, blue idle or paused, red needs you, green passed. Empty bays stay blind. Each lit lancet glows onto its sill and has a parchment tooltip on hover, focus or tap; the tooltip is fixed-position, so it stays readable on a phone. Checks shows 14 lancets (12 green, 2 red). The rose join still works on the shaded pair. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **hollow** · Shade: cocoon (Cinch). Thread spins the widget into a slim cocoon with one lit seam
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Cocoon" shade option. A point of white silk spins from the shell's outer tip in two crossing passes, cinching the boxy shell into a pointed spindle. The carapace fades under the silk, which is tethered to the iron scroll, pulls taut with a squeeze, and splits one seam open: amber while running, cold white when paused, sea-green for Checks. The name and key value sit in the seam in dark ink. Hover, keyboard focus or a tap loosens the wrap. The eyelet strap stays outside the silk, so cocooned pairs still lace. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **elven** · Shade: constellation (Distil). A silver line with star points; each star a status
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Constellation" shade option. The pane fades and star points prick out along the leaf's rim. The outline straightens into hairline chords and the night glass thins away. The figure flattens, tip first, into one silver line, and the crest's crescent drifts down to the tip. The line parts around the script name and the key value, and the status stars kindle one by one with a flare, stepping off the line into a zigzag. Jarvis has 3 white running stars (grey when paused) and 2 faint queued ones. GitDiscuss has 2 gold stars for decisions and 4 faint comment stars. Checks has 12 green and 2 gold. The joining stem stays, so shaded leaves still vine-join. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Half-coupler: gothic stone boss + felt button tab
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Stone & felt coupler" mix. Jarvis is a limestone facade, GitDiscuss and Checks are felt cushions, and each keeps its own page's display (quarry glass or cotton patch). The shared mating face is a 20 px knob. A stone corbel springs from Jarvis's buttress and ends in a carved boss with a gilded centre. A mustard felt tab hangs from GitDiscuss, box-stitched on, with a blanket-stitched buttonhole. When near, the tab perks up and the gilding warms. When ready, the tab reaches out level with its buttonhole open and the boss gets a gold ring. To join, the tab flicks up, flops over the boss, the boss pops through, only the felt squashes, and a glint runs over the boss. To release, tug the tab (or click it, or press Enter). The page's release gesture and text are now per-mix, and the felt cathedral is unchanged. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · Join: bead strand bridge (Bridge). Beads with gold spacers slide along a wire across the gap and click into place
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Bead strand bridge" join option. The pendants stay 136 px apart with an open span. Each end has a gold bead cap and crimp, which warm when near; on ready a ghost strand with empty bead rings appears. A brass wire feeds from Jarvis's cap to GitDiscuss's. Then the stones slide in from their own sides, innermost first, each with a gold spacer, overshooting a hair and clicking with a spark, and the wire sags as it loads. The stones are statuses: amber per running Jarvis agent, carnelian per GitDiscuss decision, labradorite when paused. A pearl drops into the middle with a bounce that dips the wire, and a glint runs along the strand. To release, tap the pearl (or press Enter); everything plays back in reverse. The page's join texts and release hit are now per-join. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Join: blanket stitch (Bind). A needle stitches the seam and the felt puckers; scissors snip to release
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Blanket stitch" join option. Near: tailor's chalk dashes mark each leg on both edges. Ready: a threaded needle hangs over the gap with a warm glow. Joining: the cushions butt up, the needle lowers and works down the seam one stitch per 12 fps frame (dive at the leg end, pop out at the seam, legs alternating sides) in twisted red floss. It then pulls tight (the cushions squeeze in, puckers and creases appear) and ties a French knot with a frayed tail. Release: tap the knot (hover shows gilded scissors). The scissors snip, the knot drops, the thread is drawn out of the top as one wriggling strand that whisks away, then the cushions part. The page's join texts, timing, pose and release hit are now per-join. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · Join: flying buttress (Bridge). Voussoirs drop in, keystone seats with dust; lift the keystone to release
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Flying buttress" join option. With it selected, each joining end becomes a buttress pier with its own pinnacle and a moulded corbel (the springing stone). Near: the corbels' bed joints warm with gold. Ready: a dashed ghost of the whole arch appears in the bay. Joining: the facades close to a 110 px bay, timber centring rises on a tie beam, and nine crocketed voussoirs drop in two at a time, one from each side, with a rebound and a puff of grit. The keystone falls last and seats with dust and a glint on its gilded boss, which stays warm as the join light, and then the centring is struck and drops away. The pointed arch flattens to a segment when shaded. Release: pull the keystone up (or click it, or press Enter); it lifts out and the halves slump back stone by stone, innermost first, before the facades part. The page's join texts, phases, timing and release grab are now per-join, and the rose is unchanged. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **hollow** · Join: bell and chain (Clasp). A short chain with a bell; joining rings it; tap twice to release
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Bell and chain" join option. Jarvis carries an iron chain with a brass bell, stowed in a loop between two of its strap eyelets. Near: the eyelets glint and the bell trembles as you drag. Ready: the far eyelet gets a gold dashed ring and a dotted ghost of the span appears. Joining: the shells close to a 46 px gap, the hook lifts and the chain swings across in one arc with the bell trailing, catches with a spark, drops into a sag with a bounce, and the bell rings (three swings, the clapper striking, three rings pulsing out). Joined, the bell hangs in the open seam with a warm glow as the join light. Release: tap the bell twice (the first tap rings it), pull the chain down, or press Enter twice; the bell peals, the hook lifts out and the chain swings home before the shells part. The page's join texts, phases, hold point, release timing and seam gestures are now per-join, and silk stitch is unchanged. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **elven** · Join: leaf brooch (Clasp). A leaf cloak pin descends and pins the seam; lift it to release
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Leaf brooch" join option. The casings close to a 28 px gap. Near: the stems glow and silver eyelets start to curl out. Ready: a ghost brooch hovers above a dashed outline of its landing place. Joining: the two eyelets unfurl onto one slanted line, and a frosted green-silver leaf brooch (silver rim and veins, a moonstone at the base, a hinged pin held open) drifts down swaying like a falling leaf, its shadow sharpening as it lands. The pin swings shut through both eyelets into the catch at the tip with a glint, then the moonstone kindles as the join light. Release: drag the brooch up (it rises and its pin starts to open), click it, or press Enter; it floats away and the eyelets curl back. Pulling the casings apart doesn't release it. The page's join texts, phases and release gesture are now per-join, and the vine is unchanged. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Containment: gothic reliquary bay holds a felt widget behind glass
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Reliquary bay" mix: stone Jarvis carries a colonnette and plinth stub, and felt GitDiscuss is enshrined beside it in a vault-blue niche with gilt stars. Near shows a dashed gold ghost of the shrine; ready adds ghost glass and a flickering lamp. Joining: the plinth slides out, GitDiscuss is carried in and set down with a squash, the far pier rises, a canopy of five crocketed gablets drops with dust, the glass lowers like a sash, gilt hasps turn, the lamp kindles and a gleam crosses the glass. The sheen stays off the display, and the tabs and buttons work through the glass. Release: pull the canopy up (or click it, or press Enter). The page gained an under-layer and a per-mix lift. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · Shade: coil band (Cinch). Casing compresses into a tight coiled band with a stamped plaque for name and value
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Coil band" shade option. The plate and stone fade, the frame squeezes into a narrow tube, and copper wire winds onto it from both ends with hot working ends. Each fresh turn goes on loose and cinches tight, so a ripple runs ahead of the winding, and brass end wraps lock it off. A riveted, hammered brass plaque drops on, with a bezel-set status stone (amber, labradorite, carnelian or malachite), and the name and value are stamped in left to right in dark antiqued letters. Join ends stay, so banded pendants still join. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Shade: bolster (Roll). Rolls into a stuffed tube tied with ribbon, name embroidered; unrolling bounces
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Bolster" shade option. The display is put away, then the cushion's foot curls over and the roll climbs it at 12 fps. The roll fattens as it goes, with a felt spiral turning at its end. It drops into a stuffed tube with a squash. Two satin ribbons (crimson, gold, pink) wrap it from the top down, pinching the stuffing, and their bows pop. The name is satin-stitched and the key value cross-stitched between the ribbons. Unshading unties the bows, the ribbons slip off, the roll runs down faster and faster, and the cushion slaps open with a damped bounce. Joins still work on the tubes. Not yet checked in a browser (browser access was blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · Shade: triptych closing (Fold). Wings fold over the display, grisaille name and value on their backs
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Triptych" shade option. A gilt frame comes up round the window, and two wings hinged at its top and bottom edges swing out edge-first, flashing gilded insides (tooled gold ground with a row of haloed saints). Then they fall shut over the display one after the other, casting a shadow ahead of them, with a rebound and a puff of dust. Gilt turn-buttons rotate across the seam with a glint. The wall then lowers and the frame closes in to a 64 px strip (46 for Checks). The closed wings are cool grey grisaille: the name in incised capitals between two painted statues in niches on top, and the key value on a painted banderole below. Unshading reverses it, and joins still work. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **hollow** · Shade: map roll (Roll). The parchment rolls into a thread-tied scroll with a quill label
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Map roll" shade option. The tabs and studs fade, then the parchment rolls up from its bottom edge toward its pins, with the roll fattening as it climbs and casting a shadow. The pins pop out, the shell draws in, and the scroll lands across the dorsal plate with a settle; it shows a spiral end, the paper's outer edge and faint map ink. Red thread winds twice on each side (pinching the paper) and knots, with its tails swinging. A paper slip is pasted on from the middle, and a quill darts in, writes the name and key value left to right in ink, and lifts away. The lantern still shows run state. Unshading slips the thread and unrolls the map with no quill. Joins still work. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **elven** · Shade: leaf furl (Roll). Curls into a slender leaf; the vein is a progress line, the tip the count
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Leaf furl" shade option. The pane fades, then the casing's lower edge curls over and the roll climbs the night glass, fattening and casting a shadow ahead of it. Fibres sweep round it, the silver edge rides over the top, and its open end shows the pale underside wound in a spiral that spins as it rolls. It ends as one slender night-teal furled leaf with a long tapered tip and settles with a small breath. Its midrib then lights from the base toward the tip as a progress line, with side veins lighting as it passes: Jarvis's task 12/14, GitDiscuss's queue 4/6, Checks 12/14. It glows while running and goes grey when paused. Two buds on the unlit part mark what is left (pale blue for queued, gold for decisions and approvals). The silver script name and a caption sit at the base above the vein, and a moonstone dewdrop swells at the tip holding the count. The joining stem stays, so vine and brooch joins still work. The join stem is now a shared `joinStem()` helper, and the shade's casing timing is per-shade. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Pairwise seam: wire coil-wraps a gothic pier
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Copper ivy on a pier" mix. Jarvis is stone, now with an engaged colonnette on its buttress (leaf capital, moulded base, small pinnacle). GitDiscuss and Checks are wire pendants: copper frames, coiled bezels, dark cabochon glass, bead buttons. Three copper tendrils are bound to GitDiscuss's frame. Near: they uncurl toward the pier with warm tips. Ready: dashed ghost coil bands show on the shaft. Joining: the strands leap across one at a time in hot arcs, catch with a spark and wind four turns down the shaft, passing behind the stone (drawn under Jarvis) and round the front. The working end runs hot, each fresh turn sits loose until the next one snugs it, and the strand pulls taut. Each band ties off in a curl holding a stone (peridot, amber, peridot); the amber one lights as the join light, and two wire ivy leaves open on the strands. Release: pull the wire (or click it, or press Enter); the bands unwind as you pull. In shade the pier shortens and the bands drop to two turns. The gothic display CSS now applies to any `stone*` pair. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · Angle: jump-ring pivot. Drag to set the angle; the ring turns in the loops; optional detents
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Jump-ring pivot" join option, with an Angle row (slider plus a Detents button: 15°, 30° or off) that shows only for this option. The jump ring threads and shuts as before, then GitDiscuss swings on it with a damped overshoot and settles at the set angle (default +15°). The bend is split like a chain link: the ring turns half the angle in Jarvis's loop, so its closed seam visibly rolls round, and GitDiscuss turns the other half in the ring. Drag GitDiscuss's nameplate to swing it (Jarvis still moves the pair), or focus it and press ↑ ↓. While it swings, a copper dial wire appears past its far end with a degree readout; detents are seed beads on it that catch the pendant and light amber, and on letting go it clicks into the nearest one (with detents on, the range is ±30°; off, ±40°). The pair shifts vertically to stay on the stage. Releasing (click the ring) swings it level before the ring opens. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Angle: cloth hinge. Any angle; creases on the inside corner, stretch on the outside
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Cloth hinge" join option, with an Angle row (slider from −40° to +40°, default −20°, no detents) that shows only for this option. Near: chalk marks on both edges. Ready: a ghost strip in the gap. Joining: the cushions close to an 18 px gap, a roll of oatmeal felt drops in and unrolls down the gap, and a needle tacks it on with running stitch at 12 fps, first to Jarvis and then to GitDiscuss, finishing with cross-stitch bar tacks. GitDiscuss then flops over to the angle and settles. GitDiscuss turns about the strip's inside corner, which closes up by up to 12 px. On the inside, the felt puffs out, its pinked teeth crowd together and creases fan in, one per 8° of bend, over a pinch shadow. On the outside, the same eight teeth spread wide and shallow, the felt goes pale, and stretched fibres show across it. To bend it, drag GitDiscuss's nameplate (Jarvis still moves the pair), use the slider, or press ↑ ↓; a chalk arc with 10° ticks and a pinned cotton tag with the angle in cross-stitch appear while it bends. The pair refits vertically to stay on the stage. Release: tap the strip (or press Enter). It plays back in reverse: GitDiscuss straightens, the stitches are unpicked, and the strip rolls back up. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · Angle: rose as dial. Turn the rose to set the angle; petals are detents
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Rose dial" join option, with an Angle row (slider from −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°) that shows only for this option. The facades close to a 52 px gap, and the half-roses slide out of their piers to meet in it, growing as they fuse. A wheel of twelve limestone stones (cusped inner edge, small sunk quatrefoils) is set round the rose from the bottom up with puffs of grit. A gilded pawl with a fleur-de-lis head drops into the notch between two petals with a glint, and the rose lights. Then GitDiscuss pivots on the rose's centre and swings to the angle with one heavy overshoot. With this option the joining ends stop square at the cornice (no half-gables). The wheel is sized to cover both ends, so no corner or gable tip ever visibly clashes or gapes. The rose turns twice as far as GitDiscuss, so each petal passing the pawl is one 15° detent: the pawl hops and glints at each one, including during the swing. To set the angle: drag round the rose (it catches near each detent and snaps on release), drag GitDiscuss's nameplate, use the slider, or use the arrow keys on the rose or on GitDiscuss. A parchment label under the wheel reads the angle and petal count while it turns, and the pair refits vertically to stay on the stage. Release: tap the pawl (or press Enter on the rose). GitDiscuss swings level, the pawl tips out, the wheel comes apart top first, the halves withdraw and the facades part. A code review fixed several bugs: the angle label could stick on screen; a swing could keep moving the pair after a state change; switching joins mid-drag broke the state; and the pair wasn't refitted on unshade. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **hollow** · Angle: hanging bracket. An iron bracket sets the angle; the bell or lamp hangs plumb
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Hanging bracket" join option, with an Angle row (slider from −30° to +30° in 10° steps, default +20°) that shows only for this option. The shells close to a 44 px gap. Hinge leaves reach out from the middle eyelets and meet on an iron knuckle, and its brass pin drops in with a spark. A notched iron quadrant sweeps up from Jarvis's strap and arcs over the gap. A lamp bracket (post, arm and curled brace) rises from GitDiscuss's strap through the quadrant, and a brass pawl seats in the middle notch. A lantern drops onto the arm's hook and kindles as the join light. GitDiscuss then swings on the knuckle to the angle with an overshoot. The lantern is a real pendulum (Verlet) on the moving bracket, so it lags, swings and settles plumb at any angle. The pawl rides up between notches and drops in at each one with a spark. To set the angle: swing the bracket or the lantern, drag GitDiscuss's nameplate (sticky detents, snaps on release), use the slider, or use ↑ ↓ on the focused bracket or on GitDiscuss. The pair refits vertically to stay on the stage, including while shading. Release: tap the knuckle pin (or press Enter on the bracket). GitDiscuss swings level, the pin is drawn, the lantern is lifted off, and the ironwork folds back before the shells part. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **elven** · Angle: branching. A fork in a branch; golden-angle soft detents
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Branching" join option, with an Angle row (slider from −50° to +50°, default −42.5°) that shows only for this option. Near: a birch bud swells on each stem. Ready: the buds glow and a dashed ghost fork appears. Joining: the casings close, white birch twigs with dark lenticels grow from both stems and fuse into a knot with a glint. The parent branch grows down from the knot to a curl, and five leaves open along it in phyllotaxis order (each turned 137.5° from the last, so the far ones are smaller and dimmer). The moonstone in the knot kindles, then GitDiscuss swings to its angle with one overshoot. The knot reforms to the angle: it swells toward each limb, the parent branch splits the bend evenly, bark ridges form in tight crotches, and the twigs lengthen with the bend so the casings never touch. Soft detents: the drag turns sticky near a 137.5° fork (the golden angle, ±42.5° tilt), 144° (⅖ turn, ±36°) and a straight bough, and the moonstone brightens near them (warm gold at the golden angle). Anywhere between is a valid resting place. To bend: drag GitDiscuss's nameplate, use the slider, or press ↑ ↓ on the nameplate or the knot to step between growth angles (Shift+↑ ↓ for 1°). While it bends, a hairline dial under the knot (the parent branch is its needle) shows the detents as stars, and a moonstone tag reads the fork angle. The pair refits vertically to stay on the stage. Release: click the knot (or press Enter); everything plays back in reverse. A code review fixed several bugs: the dial could stick on screen; an angle animation fought drags of the pair; changing the angle mid-join split the fork; and the dial drew over GitDiscuss. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Layer stack: silver filigree (wire material, elven ornament)
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Silver filigree" mix on a night-blue stage. All three widgets are elven leaves rebuilt in round silver wire of one gauge (wire can't taper, so the swell becomes granules and coils). Each is an openwork frame of two wires with a channel of tiny alternating scrolls between them, tied with coil bindings, on a blackened backplate. They have wire tendril tips with enamelled wire leaves, a moonstone-glazed wire crescent crest, a moonstone drop on a jump ring, and the pointed moonstone pane in a coiled bezel, with script names. The joining end is a twisted-wire stem with two knops (moonstone beads in coiled caps). Join: the vine in silver wire. The springs on the knops uncoil when near; when ready the tips touch with a glint and a dashed ghost of the twist appears. The wires then grow across with glinting ends and twist over and under, coiling round the far knops. Two moonstone beads ride out and spring into place, four plique-à-jour wire leaves open, and a five-petal filigree flower blooms; its moonstone kindles as the join light. Release: pull the flower (or click it, or press Enter). A "plain narrow, in silver" baseline shade is included. A code review fixed several issues: knop caps were drawn over the wire coils; the knobs had no keyboard focus ring; the knobs collided with the bezel; and the shaded Checks pill overlapped its frame wires. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · Join: coil wrap (Bind). A wire end leaps across and wraps the other frame three times; grab a band to unwind
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Coil wrap" join option. Each joinable end now has a spine: a bar of heavy wire bent out from the frame on two elbows. Jarvis has three curled wire ends. Near: the curls open and reach, and the spines warm. Ready: the tips touch GitDiscuss's spine and dashed ghost coils appear. Joining: the ends leap across in high arcs and hook GitDiscuss's spine with a spark. They draw taut and reel GitDiscuss in until the spines lie side by side. Then each end winds five turns round both spines, behind and in front, with a hot working end and loose turns that cinch tight. The ends tuck into downward curls, and the middle curl holds an amber bead, which is the join light. A wave of heat anneals the bands. Release: grab a band and pull; it unwinds with the pull, lets go past halfway, and winds back on if you stop short. A click or Enter also releases it. In shade there are two turns per band, and the spines and roots follow the squeezed frame in the bead strand and coil band shades. A code review found no syntax errors; its shade-geometry, curl-placement and screen-reader fixes are applied. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Join: button tab (Clasp). A felt tab flops over and buttons on, overshoots and settles; tug to release
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Button tab" join option. A raspberry felt tab, box-stitched to Jarvis's end, hangs limp; GitDiscuss has a horn coat button cross-stitched on. Near: the tab perks up and the button stands up. Ready: the tab reaches out level with its buttonhole gaping, and a ghost ring marks the landing. Joining (12 fps): the cushions close, the tab flicks up and back, flops over with an overshoot, the button pops through the stretched hole, both cushions squash, fibres shake loose and the tail wobbles and settles. Release: tug the tab. The felt stretches and drags the button, puckering GitDiscuss, until it pops free (let go early and it holds), or click it or press Enter. The button slips back, the tab drops and swings like a pendulum, then the cushions part. The engine gained a generic `tug` release gesture. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · Join: lead came solder (Bind). One shared window; molten solder runs the came and cools; a knife cracks it open
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Lead came solder" join option. Past a stone mullion beside its display, each joining end has half of a pointed centre light: dark quarry glass with diamond leading, a pieced ruby and blue border, half a hood moulding, and a bare lead came edge with its channel open. Near: a gleam slides down the open cames and the glass warms. Ready: a hot soldering iron hangs over the seam, and dashed rings mark the joints. Joining: the facades close into one window under one hood, and the iron comes down to the apex. A bead of solder runs down the came, pausing to puddle at each joint where the diamond leading meets it, with sparks and flux smoke. Behind it, the trail glows white, yellow and orange and cools through dull red to grey from the top down. The iron lifts away, a tin glint runs down the seam, and light comes through the centre light and spills onto the stone and floor; this light is the join light. Release: grab the seam and pull down to draw a glazier's knife (hovering shows it). Each joint cracks with flecks of solder as the blade passes, the came splits, the light goes out and the facades part; let go before halfway and it holds. A click or Enter also releases it. Shaded, the centre light becomes a short soldered slit. A code review found no syntax errors. Its fixes are applied: the knife no longer jumps to the grab point, the shaded slit now keeps its glass, there is now a real mullion between the hood and the display, the spill no longer washes the displays, and the ready iron stays clear of the status line. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).

Felt Checks docks into the bay, and a ripple from the touch point turns it into a limestone predella with plum quarry glass (its felt colour) and its ✤ glyph. - [x] **wire** · Morph: tree growth. Cabochon, then branch with two displays, then hoop tree; beads slide out to new tips
  - Added to `wire.html` as a new "Morph" row (Tree growth), with a Checks size row: Pendant, Cabochon, Branch and Hoop tree buttons, a growth slider and the M key. Pendant to cabochon: the frame closes in on the stone, the coils wind round it from the bottom up, a bail and a slab name tag appear, and the status lens rides up to the bail. Cabochon to branch: a five-wire rope trunk grows down into an amethyst nugget, the stone slides out along one branch, and a second branch reaches out to coil a bezel and fill a second display (12 / 14 and 02 waiting). Branch to hoop tree: the roots fan out and wrap onto a copper hoop that draws round from the bottom, a crown display grows up, and 14 twigs push out from behind the displays with a bead riding each tip (12 malachite, 2 carnelian). Every frame is drawn from one growth value, so shrinking plays it backwards. Checks moves below the pair when there is room, a shaded tree folds back to its cabochon, and dragging uses each form's real bounds. A code review found no load-order errors, overlaps or text overflow. Its fixes are applied: Checks now always lands exactly back on the pendant, shrinking a shaded tree no longer stalls, the slider unshades the same way the size buttons do, the stage bounds now keep up with the hoop, M steps up from a scrubbed size, and Esc or the arrow keys during a morph are no longer overridden. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Morph: stuffing. Growing inflates like a cushion; shrinking deflates with wrinkles; squash on landing
  - Added to `felt.html` as a new "Morph" row (Stuffing), with a Checks size row: Pad, Cushion and Pillow buttons, a size slider and the M key. Growing (12 fps): a slit opens in the side seam, and three cream wool tufts fly in and are pushed through it. Each gulp swells the felt with an overshoot, bowing the sides out. The slit is cross-stitched shut, then Checks hops and lands with a squash that shakes fibres loose. The cushion has a bigger cotton display with the count in cross-stitch, and a row of 14 French knots under it (12 green, 2 amber). The pillow adds the run and pass rate, a cross-stitch bar chart and the two waiting checks by name, and wool pompoms pop out at its corners. Shrinking: the slit opens and the stuffing puffs out, the felt sags inward with creases fanning from the corners and edges, the name band and display ride down with the sag, then it is stitched shut and settles. Each display fades in only once the patch is at full size, so text never clips. Checks moves below the pair when there is room. Shading a grown Checks deflates it first, and unshading refills it. A code review found no load-order errors. Its fixes are applied: display text could clip for a frame, scrubbing back and forth (or reversing a morph midway) made the size jump, the slider snapped the pair out of shade, moves during a morph were lost, the size restored after unshading could be wrong, and the sagging band slid under the display. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).

The sheet is one corner of a larger map, with the rest rolled along its bottom edge (tied with red thread) and folded under on the right. Atlas: the shell widens, the fold lifts, and the folded half swings out from behind in CSS 3D perspective, slapping flat with a puff of paper dust; it names deploy-preview and e2e-safari beside matching red rings, with a compass rose, and the crease stays. Every frame comes from one growth value, so shrinking rolls and folds it back. Checks grows from where it stands and only moves to stay on the stage and clear of the pair. - [x] **elven** · Morph: growth. A branch extends and a leaf opens with a new display; shrinking is autumn
  - Added to `elven.html` as a new "Morph" row (Growth), with a Checks size row: Bud, Sprig and Bough buttons, a size slider and the M key. Sprig: a birch stem pushes out from under the bud's moonstone drop, a silver tendril curls off it, a bud swells at the node, and a broad night-glass leaf unfurls beneath (it lengthens, opens from its midrib in pale green, swings down with an overshoot and darkens). Its moonstone pane lights with a glint: run 418 on main, 86% passed, twelve stars and two open rings. Bough: the branch grows on out of the bud's far tip, leafing as it passes and ending in a fiddlehead, and a third leaf unfurls from a node to name deploy-preview and e2e-safari waiting on Brent. Shrinking is autumn: the panes dim, the leaves turn gold, fold on their midribs, let go and flutter down, and the branch draws back into the bud (scrubbing the slider down plays autumn too). Checks grows from where it stands and only moves to stay on the stage and clear of the pair. Shading a grown Checks lets its leaves fall first, and unshading grows them again. A code review found no load-order errors. Its fixes are applied: the HTML panes are now clipped to their pointed bezels, the Checks nameplate no longer stretches across the bough, the autumn pane face now dims, the glint now sits on the bezel, Esc after a morph re-clamps Checks, and the empty part of the grown box no longer blocks clicks. Near: the tendril starts to unfurl and the spring's mouth flares and warms. Ready: the tendril is almost straight and a dashed ghost runs it through the coil. Joining: the tendril unfurls into a straight bar and slides into the spring as the casings close. The spring then winds shut a turn at a time from the frame out, twisting as it goes; each turn overshoots tight, and the hot working turn passes behind and in front of the bar. Then a spark, two leaves open at the coil's mouth, and the collar kindles as the join light. Release: pull the coil (or click it, or press Enter). In shade the coupler keeps its fixed size on the centre line. The wire display CSS now applies to any `*wire` pair. A code review's fixes are applied: the hot turn was drawn over the bar, the buttons had no focus ring, the ready glow dropped out on the first joining frame, a rejoin mid-release jumped, the heat flickered, and the tendril could poke past a close coil. - [x] **wire** · Shade: hoop rim (Sink). Contents retract into the frame wire; an empty outline with a single bead readout
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Hoop rim" shade option. First the display sinks: the plate drops back and darkens, the screen fades, the bezel coils unwind, the scroll curls draw in, and the inner wire swells out until it lies under the frame. The frame then closes to a strip and stays as an empty copper outline with the stage showing through. A single glass bead threads on at the left corner and slides along the bottom rim to the right, settling with a spark. It carries the value, and its core glows with the status: amber, labradorite, carnelian or malachite. Last, a hot wire point writes the name in copper lettering. Unshading plays it all backwards, and the join ends stay. A code review's fixes are applied: the display's scale jumped in 10% steps, and the bead's overshoot carried it onto the join hardware. - [x] **felt** · Shade: drawstring (Cinch). Casing gathers into ruffles; pull the toggle to open
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Drawstring" shade option. The name band slides down to the middle and becomes the casing. Two buttonhole-stitched eyelets open, and a twisted cord grows out of them through a turned wooden toggle, ending in felt balls. Frilled scallops form above and below the band, with creases leaning toward the eyelets, a hem stitch following the frills and the sides pinching in. It lands with a squash and loose fibres. The bottom drag clamp allows for the hanging cord.   - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Hanging sign" shade option. The tabs, studs and nameplate fade, and the parchment crossfades into a pale plank. The plank shrinks to fit its words while the carapace draws in and fades, leaving the iron scroll, the lantern and the eyelet strap. An iron bar runs out from the scroll, white-hot at its tip, dropping curled braces as it passes. It strikes the strap with a spark (Checks ends in a curled finial). Two link chains drop, and the board falls onto them with a bounce and swings. The board shows the name and one value in ink and red paint inside a painted red border, at 12 px. The swing is a damped pendulum on two equal chains, so the board stays level and readable. It swings when its value changes (the run state) and sways when the shells are dragged or joined, driven by their acceleration. It stops fully at rest. Tap a board (or press Enter on its nameplate) to pause or resume the widget: the lantern dims and Jarvis's board repaints to "agents paused". Tapping Checks knocks it. Joins still work.   - Added to `felt.html` as the "Woven label" shade option. The cushion deflates to a 46 px felt band (40 px for Checks) with a squash and loose fibres. Warp is strung from the left, then a wooden shuttle runs the weft back and forth a row per pass at 12 fps, and the jacquard name, key value and three tiny care symbols grow with the cloth. Each widget has its own label: cream and red for Jarvis, navy and gold for GitDiscuss, black and ivory for Checks. The woven border stripes are the status colour. The fringed ends fold under, machine stitch tacks them down, and a sheen crosses the satin. Unshading unpicks the tacks and pulls the weft out from the bottom up in one kinked thread, then the cushion refills with a bounce. Reviewer's fixes applied: the unweave thread now lies over the unwoven warp and is drawn off the side instead of vanishing; the fringe now shows over the felt, with no per-row jump; there are fewer rows with a longer timeline, so the shuttle visibly travels; the shadow fades in; the shuttle stays on the widget; and the label covers the name band's edge. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · Shade: inscription drum. A carved band rotates to show successive lines
  - Built in `gothic.html` as "Inscription drum": the wall lowers to its frieze (name), a channel opens, and a CSS-3D six-face limestone drum on gilt spindles spins in and seats on the current line (number in red); click, tap or arrow keys turn it a line, synced with the widget's screen page, with lozenges marking the line. It never turns by itself. Not checked in a browser (browser and node were blocked this run).
- [x] **hollow** · Shade: fog sink (Sink). Sinks behind a fog bank; spire tips and lantern glow remain
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Fog sink" shade option: fog rolls in at the foot from both ends, iron spires sprout from the ridge, and the shell sinks into the bank (its foot stays put) until only the spire tips and iron scroll show against a paler mist, the lantern glowing through the fog; spire slits kindle as status lights (amber/cold per Jarvis agent or GitDiscuss decision, Checks 3 teal + 1 red), and the name and value are inked on the densest fog. The strap stays clear, so joins work. Reviewer (reading only) found no load errors; its fixes are applied (the shell copy covered the fading parchment on the first frame, unclamped drag origin, ink-bleed fade). Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **elven** · Join: quicksilver (Fuse). Liquid silver edges meet, ripple and seal into one rim
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Quicksilver" join option: the stems melt into chrome liquid drawn through an SVG goo filter (blur and alpha threshold, specular light from the backdrop moon), with the knops floating on it; near beads bulge and reach, ready hangs them a hair apart under a ghost rim; joining snaps them together with a flash, zips the seam with an overshoot, splashes four drops that are pulled back, rings ripples, settles into one rim with a meniscus, sweeps a glint, and the moon's crescent shows in the silver as the join light. Release: pull a casing away (the rim necks; past SNAP it pinches off, sooner it draws the casing back), click the rim or press Enter; the halves recoil with the bridge's own profile and set. The engine's pull messages are now per join. Reviewer's fixes applied (ready→joining blink, bead inflation at the pinch, the jitter-only tremble, the near-edge pop). Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Layer stack: felt bugs (wire structure in felt material)
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Felt bugs" mix on a moss baize: wire-page pendants made in felt (copper-wool cord frames with gold whip-stitched wraps, scroll curls, couched cream bezels round cotton displays, felt-ball buttons, eye loops, a wool-bead meter, a ladybird on Checks). Join "Caterpillar jump ring": a button-eyed felt caterpillar sleeps as an open ring through Jarvis's loop, rears when near, swoops its head through GitDiscuss's loop (over/under both loops) and bites its tail at 12 fps; French-knot status spots pop on as the join light; pull its head (or click, or Enter) to release. "Plain tuck, in felt" baseline shade. Reviewer (reading only) found no load errors; its fixes are applied (Checks nameplate vs cords, clipped loop arcs, rejoin snap, squash drift, one-frame flop). - [x] **wire** · Join: nest (Grow). Fine wires tangle across the gap around a small cabochon; snap back with a twang on release
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Wire nest" join option (gap 64): fourteen seeded fine wires (copper from Jarvis, brass from GitDiscuss) bound into tufts that bristle when near; ready shows a dashed ghost nest and stone; joining grows them out with hot tips, looping wildly round the gap until the sides tangle (some hook the far frame), the tangle draws in, a pyrite-flecked lapis cabochon drops and bounces, a fine brass coil winds round it and a curl unrolls below; the stone's halo is the join light. Release (tap the stone or Enter) plays its own sequence rather than ungrowing: the coil unwinds and the stone lifts out, the loops pull taut and hum as the pendants part, the wires snap mid-gap with sparks and whip home with a damped twang; scrubbing down from 100% plays it too. Reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws; its fixes are applied (release latch reset on join switch and hold, no sideways jump at the snap, free-ended strands whip home whole). Not yet checked in a browser (browser, node and python access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Join: velcro (Fuse). Press together with a squash; fibres stretch and snap on release — felt.html "Velcro": hook/loop tapes bristle when near, hard squash with strike marks and fibre puff; tap the seam to peel: slow stretching creep, 16 fibres thin, tent and snap one by one, then a jolt apart (not checked in a browser this run: no browser or node access)
- [x] **gothic** · Join: frieze procession (Fuse, decorative). Carved figures walk across the seam, then freeze
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Frieze procession" join option (gap 0, square joining ends). Each facade has a frieze band of pointed niches on colonnettes under cresting, above the nameplate. Eight carved, hooded, faceless figures stand in Jarvis's niches: cross-bearer, censer, banner, crowned king with orb, monk with book, lantern, pilgrim with crook, acolyte with candle. GitDiscuss's niches are empty. Near: the figures stir and the empty niches warm. Ready: their places are chalked in GitDiscuss's niches. Joining: the gabled end blocks sink, a foliage boss drops onto the seam with grit, and a roll moulding runs down the joint. The figures wake from the front, walk four niches across the seam in single file (bobbing gait, censer swinging, banner trailing, lantern and candle lit), then freeze leader first, four on each side of the boss, and their niches light as the join light (amber, or blue when paused). Release: drag the procession left (past halfway they go home; they turn, walk back, turn and freeze), or click it, or press Enter. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. I applied its fixes: the chalk outlines no longer pop in, the drag now walks the figures step for step with the pointer, the boss sits lower so walkers barely cross it, the band's shadow stays off the nameplate, and a missing space in the hint is fixed. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **hollow** · Join: mycelium (Grow). Pale fungal threads reach across and knit together
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Mycelium" join option (40 px gap, slow). Near: pale fuzz sprouts on both straps and stirs while you drag. Ready: a dotted ghost of the threads. Joining: fourteen seeded bone-white threads with bright tips creep out of the straps, branch, and take hold of the far strap with small nodes. The web then draws taut over a ragged cottony mat, with fog showing through. Three mushrooms push up from its top edge and their caps open with an overshoot. A pulse of light runs in from both straps and leaves the web faintly lit: foxfire green while both run, cold blue-white if either is paused. A few spores drift up from the big cap. Nothing moves at rest. Release: pull the web down to stretch it and snap threads one at a time; let go and it springs back while the broken threads grow together (Esc does the same). Tearing it, a tap or Enter wilts the mushrooms, snaps the rest with puffs of spores, and withers every thread back into its strap. Reviewer (reading only) found no must-fix items. Its polish is applied: tighter growth timing so every thread lands before the knit, a smooth Esc, no empty paths, anchor nodes kept on broken threads, and a few threads left to snap during a tear. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Material bleed: stone turns to felt across the seam via a noise mask
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Material bleed" mix. Jarvis is stone and GitDiscuss and Checks are felt, with no hardware between them. Join "Moss and frost" (Fuse, gap 0): near, fibres lift off the felt edge and grit drifts off the buttress; ready shows a faint ghost of the bleed. Joining: the casings butt with a felt squash and a puff of fibres and grit. Then, in a 46 px zone each side of the seam, one noise-plus-distance field (an SVG turbulence mask, thresholded) decides the material. Teal felt spreads over the buttress in puffed colonies like moss, with the cushion's edge stitch running through it. A raised limestone crust (lit edge, cast shadow, Jarvis's ashlar joints carried on) creeps over the felt like frost. They meet on one ragged line that fills the notch, and three gold French knots set on the seam as the join light. Release: pull the seam (or click, or Enter); the knots go, both materials draw back with fibres and chips falling, and the casings part. The zone stops short of every display and nameplate. Reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. Its fixes are applied: blur after the mask so the shadows are soft, the top notch filled, no hairline crack on the seam, joints in line with Jarvis's coursing, and no stitch fork. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · Angle: bent-wire joint. The connecting wire bends with a visible radius and a locking coil at the bend
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Bent wire" join option (gap 64, −40° to +40°, default +30°, shares the Angle row and detents). It has copper and brass ferrules on bail wires. A twisted square brass rod feeds across with a hot tip and threads in with a spark. An 11-turn copper coil winds loose round the middle and tucks into curls. Then GitDiscuss bends: the rod is filleted round a 28 px radius, the outer face of the bend glints, and the coil turns crowd on the inside of the curve. The coil cinches tight with a glint to lock it, and it slackens and runs hot whenever the angle is being changed. Bend it with the nameplate, the slider or ↑ ↓ on the coil; tap the coil to release. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Morph: crescent bend. The capsule bends continuously into a crescent
  - Added to `felt.html` as a third morph option, "Crescent bend" (sizes Pad, Arc, Crescent; slider and M follow). The casing is one felt bolster drawn from a single bend value: a centreline arc whose sweep, length and thickness grow while its end profile goes from squared-off to round to pointed horns, so it bends continuously. Growing (12 fps): the display goes, the pad crouches, bends with an overshoot and lands rocking on its belly (rotating and rolling). Creases bunch on the inside of the bend and the outside stretches pale. Arc: the count and run in the belly, and 14 French knots sewn along the ends (two amber at the right tip). Crescent: the ends curl past level into tapered horns, a bigger count with 86%, the knots ride up the horns, deploy-preview and e2e-safari are stitched along the horns, and a felt star sits on each tip. Shrinking unpicks the stars or knots first, then springs open past flat. The name band stays straight under the inside edge, so it never leaves the felt. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. Its fixes are applied: the stitch line now insets at the ends too, the flat pose draws the pad's own casing so the crouch doesn't pop, the rock is now a true roll (it slid twice as far before), and creases no longer show as slivers under the band. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · Angle: fan vault. Ribs fan out to fill the inside of tight angles
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Fan vault" join option (gap 0, −60° to +60°, 15° stops, default +30°): GitDiscuss turns about the corner it shares with Jarvis (bottom when bending down, top when bending up) and a limestone fan fills the wedge, a rib peeling off GitDiscuss's end every 7.5° with rings, cusped panels, a crested rim and an outer band of glass lights (the join light: amber and ruby, blue if paused); a gilded springer boss sits on a corbel (rising fan) or a pinnacle (pendant fan). Near warms the springer corners, ready chalks the fan; drag the fan or nameplate, slider or arrows; tap the boss to release. Reviewer's fixes applied (right-edge overhang, ready glow drop, drags no longer cross level, fixed-pitch cresting, limit message). Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · Join: overlapping hoops (Interlock). Rings overlap into linked circles; the lens between them is the status lamp
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Overlapping hoops" join option (gap 96). Each joinable end carries a heavy hoop on a short banded stem: copper on Jarvis, and split brass on GitDiscuss with a fine cut. Each hoop has a fine coil of the other metal round its lower curve. Near: both hoops warm and the brass cut cracks open, showing gold ball ends. Ready: a dashed ghost of the brass hoop where it will overlap. Joining: the cut springs wide and the brass hoop turns edge-on on its stem while the pendants close in. It then swings face-on, its open end sweeping through the copper ring, and the cut twists shut with a spark. The rings are linked, with copper over brass at the top crossing and under it at the bottom. The vesica lens between them fills with glass from the bottom up with a settling surface, kindles and glints. It is the status lamp: the left half is Jarvis and the right half GitDiscuss, amber while running and labradorite when paused. Tap the lamp (or press Enter) to unlink; the sequence plays backwards. The hoops shrink with the shade, so shaded strips still link. Reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. Its fixes are applied: a visible stem at full height (smaller hoops), ring shadows kept off the glass, the top crossing covered from first contact, a brass stem on GitDiscuss, and a lens-shaped focus ring. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Join: mitten hands (Grow, character). Two felt mittens reach out and clasp, stop-motion
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Mitten hands" join option (gap 56): box-stitched felt sleeves with knitted mittens (red Jarvis, indigo GitDiscuss, ribbed cream cuffs, cross-stitch snowflakes) hang at rest, lift when near and hold out thumbs-up with a ghost handshake when ready; at 12 fps they slide past each other, both thumbs clamp over with a squeeze and a fibre puff, shake three times (cushions jiggle), and the snowflakes are restitched gold cross by cross as the join light. Release: tug the handshake (arms stretch, grip slips; short tugs hold), click or Enter; they let go, wave goodbye and drop with a swing before the cushions part. No face stitches, to keep it from tipping into childish. Reviewer's fixes applied (sleeve filter clipping when level, arm reach in short shades and under a tug, re-grip as a tug fades, thumb layer swap). Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **gothic** · FIX (feedback): the "Nave" size of Build by bays grew over GitDiscuss
  - Every brainstorm item was already built, so this run took the open feedback item. In `gothic.html`, when a grown Checks has no free side (a 420×306 nave beside a pair spread across the stage), it takes the floor and the pair is eased up just far enough to clear it, with 20 px of headroom for the finial and gin wheel; the pair moves while Checks slides over, before the scaffold goes up. Shrinking waits until the walls are down, then settles the pair back. With the default layout the pair rises 79 px, to y 126. This applies to both morphs and the slider, and to the pair whether separate, joined or mid-join. A pair you move by hand stays where you put it, and dropping Checks elsewhere lets the pair back down. Reviewer's fixes applied: a guard missed a pair standing low, a pending settle could be lost to a drag, and turning the rose dial froze the lift. Known limit: an angled dial or apse joint is measured unrotated, so a steeply swung GitDiscuss can still meet the nave. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **wire** · FIX (feedback): a grown Checks (hoop tree, slab) could grow over the pair

Checks now moves to the nearest side with room (below, beside or above). When none has room (a hoop tree beside a pair standing between about y 194 and 312), Checks takes the floor and the pair rises just enough to clear it before the tree grows, then settles back as Checks shrinks. This covers both morphs, the slider, joins in progress, angled joins (GitDiscuss's swing is measured) and unshading a shaded tree. A pair moved by hand stays put, and moving Checks away lets the pair back down. Reviewer's fixes applied: the slider stalled the lift, a morph ending mid-swing or mid-unwind left the pair raised, the pair was measured shaded or without its swing, and join or separate never rechecked the room. - [x] **wire** · Shade: hoop rim for hoop widgets. The tree retracts into the rim, branches fold down, beads gather; the strip is an arc of the rim with its wraps and one bead lit
  - Every listed item was built, and the one unbuilt part of a brainstorm idea was the hoop-widget half of Hoop rim (only the capsule half existed). Added to `wire.html` as a new "Hoop rim · tree" shade option; "Hoop rim" is unchanged. Clicking it grows Checks into a hoop tree, and Jarvis and GitDiscuss shade as in Hoop rim. Shading the tree (2.6 s): the displays go dark and their coils unwind, the 14 check beads slide down their twigs and drop onto the bottom of the rim with a click, the branches draw in, the trunk sinks, the root wraps unwind and the roots draw back into the rim. The empty hoop then opens at the top with hot ends and relaxes into a shallow arc that rises into the strip. Strip: *Checks*, 12 malachite and 2 carnelian beads, *12/14*. Unshading plays it back. A size change, the slider or a mid-shade change brings the tree back up first. Reviewer's fixes applied: the rim tilts had the wrong sign, the pair could drop onto a regrowing hoop, a pending rise was cancelled, there were jumps when the size changed mid-shade, and the layout was too tight. - [x] **combined** · Layer stack: felt cathedral's flying buttress, built from felt voussoirs that flop into place (from the layer-stack model's notes)
  - Every listed item was built; the brainstorm's layer-stack notes name one more join ("a felt cathedral's flying buttress is built from felt voussoirs that flop into place"). Added to `combined.html` as the "Felt flying buttress" mix, the felt cathedral's second join (its half-rose mix is unchanged). `fcCasing` gained a `pier` option: the joining ends become buttress piers with felt pinnacles, a blind lancet and a stitched set-off. Felt corbels sit on the pier faces. Near: their gold knots swell and their stitch turns gold. Ready: the whole arch is tacked out in white basting. Joining (12 fps): the kneelers close to a 110 px bay, a tailor's chalk line marks the curve, and stuffed wedges (each widget's own wool) drop in two at a time from the springings up, falling limp and tilted, flopping level with an overshoot and squashing as they land. The wine-red keystone lands with a puff of fluff and the kneelers give. A running stitch then binds the ring, French-knot crockets pop on, and the keystone's appliqué quatrefoil is sewn on lobe by lobe round a gold knot, which is the join light. Shaded, the arch flattens to a segment. Release: pull the keystone up (or click, or Enter); the stitching comes out, it lifts away, and the wedges flop off the ring innermost first. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. I applied its must-fix: the shaded segment's springers floated over flat corbels, so the corbels now have a sloped seat. I also applied three of its polish fixes: a chalk dot on the first frame, inaccurate join text, and wedge colours now taken from the kneeler palette. Not yet checked in a browser (browser, node and python access were blocked this run).
- [x] **felt** · Join: mitten hands with the optional face stitches (character variant)
  - Every listed item was built; the mitten-hands idea's "optional face stitches turn widgets into characters" was the one unbuilt part. Added to `felt.html` as a new "Mitten friends" join option ("Mitten hands" is unchanged). Each cushion gets a stitched face in the plain felt at its outer end, clear of the display: felt-bead eyes with catch-lights, thread brows and a backstitched mouth. It is awake while the widget runs, and asleep with stitched z's when paused. When near, the faces look round at each other; when ready, they gasp. From the clasp on, they grin with their eyes squeezed shut and pink felt cheeks pressed on. Under a tug they look worried and follow the pointer. As they part, Jarvis winks while they wave, and they go glum, then ease back to rest. Faces fade under any shade. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. Its fixes are applied: the cheeks now clear the display's dashed outline, the brows no longer vanish for a frame, there is no pop from glum to neutral, and the cheeks no longer jump back at let-go. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Dominant skin the other way: stone Checks turns to felt in a felt group, plus the "make my groups match" preference (from Model C's notes)
  - Every listed item was built; Model C's notes left two parts unbuilt: "the group being dropped onto" wins in either direction, and "C as a user preference (make my groups match)". Added to `combined.html` as the "Turned to felt" mix ("Turned to stone" is unchanged), with a new Groups row (Match my groups / Keep each skin, or press G). Jarvis and GitDiscuss are two felt kneelers whipstitched together at a double pier, with a felt oriel bay under the seam: a stuffed lintel with scalloped cusps, roll colonnettes with French-knot capitals, blind arcading couched in cream cord, quilted courses, and a pom-pom pendant with a gold sequin and a red tassel. Checks arrives as a limestone predella with green quarry glass. Near: gold knots are sewn onto the arch heads. Ready: tacking stitches mark the seat and felt creeps onto the stone. Joining (12 fps): Checks seats with a squash and lintel fluff, then wool spreads from the touch point with a running stitch following the edge, turning the limestone into the felt cathedral's Checks kneeler (green glass becomes its green frieze band, ✤ kept); the sequin catches the light as the join light. With "Keep each skin", Checks docks and stays stone. Switching the setting while docked sews the felt on or unpicks it in place. Release: tug the tassel (or click, or Enter); the felt unpicks back to the touch point, shedding tufts. `stoneChecks` takes a palette, `orPaint` defers to a mix's `paint`, and a mix can set its own `below` room. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. Its fixes are applied: unpick tufts now start on Checks instead of off the edge, unmatching while docked now unpicks rather than looking like sewing, the felted kneeler's pinnacles no longer pierce the lintel, there is more room below for the tassel, the stone display glows when lit, and a stale class is cleared. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).
- [x] **combined** · Half-coupler: a hinge face with an angle range (elven moon dial + Hollow iron arm), from Model A's "only Bridge, Clasp and Hinge kinds work" and "a fixed size, position and angle range"
  - Every listed item was built. The three couplers so far are a clasp, a grip and a bridge, and none of them turns, so Model A's hinge kind and its angle range were the unbuilt part. This also pairs elven with Hollow for the first time. Added to `combined.html` as the "Moon dial on an iron arm" mix, with a new Angle row (slider −30° to +30° in 10° stops, default +20°) that shows only for this mix. It defines a fourth face, the pivot: a pin on the centre line in the middle of a 44 px gap. The swing is part of the face: ±30° is the most at which two 130 px casings clear each other. Jarvis is elven night glass. It has a silver crescent on a bail from its stem, set with seven moonstones, one per stop. GitDiscuss and Checks are Hollow shells (Checks is tucked under Jarvis, clear of the swing). GitDiscuss has a brush-ink iron arm riveted to its strap, with the pale needle riveted along it as the pointer. Near: the crescent opens, the stones twinkle and the arm lifts. Ready: the arm is poised, the stone for the set angle glows, and ghosts show the ring and the arm on the pin. Joining: the crescent slowly closes into a ring with a glint, and spokes grow to a hub. The arm darts up onto the hub with a spark, a brass pin is driven in, and red silk winds round it and drops a knotted tail that hangs plumb. GitDiscuss then swings on the pin to the angle, overshoots and settles, and the stone under the needle kindles (warm while both run, blue if paused). To set the angle: drag the arm or needle (it rests at stops and the light dims between them), use the slider, or press ↑ ↓ on the arm or pin. The pair moves up or down only as far as it must to stay on the stage. Release: pull the pin (or click it, or press Enter). Engine: a per-mix `turn` hook rotates a widget about a pin, home layouts apply to any mix that has one, and hidden rows now actually hide (`.row[hidden]`, which also fixes the Held and Groups rows showing on every mix). Reviewer's fixes applied: the arm jumped or flipped side when grabbed, the Angle row never hid, a mix switch mid-swing left the angle between stops, the swing overshoot almost touched Jarvis, the arm and silk hung below a shaded shell, Hollow's scroll could reach the top line at −30°, focus was lost, the touch strip was thin, and a status message was overwritten. Growing widens the stone before cutting the new level; shrinking drops the finer level first. The phase line shows the size as a share of the full rose. The pane adds 86% and the two checks waiting on Brent. Shrinking sheds the finest level first, then draws in. Everything is a function of g. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. - [x] **combined** · Layer stack: the motion layer swapped on its own, with the builder's requires/provides check, from Model B's "stop-motion motion on elven growth looks broken, not stylised" and "each layer must declare requires and provides … the builder offers only valid stacks or a declared fallback"
  - Every listed item was built, and no mix had yet swapped the motion layer by itself or shown the builder refusing a stack. Added to `combined.html` as the "Motion layer" mix, right after Silver filigree, with a new Motion row (or press V). The stack, casing, vine join and shade are Silver filigree's, drawn by the same code. Only the timing changes: each motion is a time map over the join's phases (springs, reach, three strokes of twist, leaves, flower), over the release, the shade and the leaves' travel, plus how they seat. ✓ Growing (the stack's own motion). ✓ Springy (wire): every step overshoots and rings back, with the travel overshoot capped at 14 px. ✓ Heavy (gothic): each step gathers speed, stops dead and pauses, and the leaves lift, travel and set down with a squash. ✓ Darting (Hollow): the wires cross in three quick strokes with pauses between. ✕ Stop-motion (felt) is refused because silver can't hold a pose between frames; it is shown anyway (12 fps redraws, the felt boil shaking the leaves, a seat squash) so the reason can be seen. ↺ Its declared fallback steps only the travel, shade and seat at 12 fps, while the growth stays smooth. The layer table's Motion row shows each one's requires/provides. Picking a motion plays the join in it (releasing first if joined). The scrubber stays on raw time, so scrubbing shows the map. Engine: `setJt` takes a per-mix `travel`. Reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws. Its fixes are applied: the spring overshoot could run a casing into its partner or off the stage (now capped in px), reversing mid-travel or mid-shade jumped between the forward and reverse maps (the direction is now fixed per path and per shade run), and Growing now shades in filigree's 600 ms. Not yet checked in a browser (browser and node access were blocked this run).

  - Every listed item was built and elven had waited longest, so this fills its missing join kind. Added to `elven.html` as the "Crescent pivot" join option, with the Angle row (−50° to +50°, default −30°). Silver straps shaped like willow leaves, with opposed scrolls, grow from the stems. Jarvis's ends in a crescent whose horns grow round, and GitDiscuss's ends in a moonstone disc that swells into the crescent's arms. A star then falls from the backdrop moon and strikes the disc, staying as the pin. The moonstone kindles, a glint runs to both horn tips, and GitDiscuss turns on the pin. The angle is shown as a moon phase on the moonstone: full when level, waxing when raised, waning when lowered. The seven phases are soft stops 15° apart, and the horns are the hard stops. While it turns, a silver pointer hangs over an arc of seven small phase moons, and a tag shows the angle beside a moon in its phase. The pin sits a fixed span from each stem, so turning never moves GitDiscuss along. To make the hooks for this, the page's angle code (pivot offset, stops, names, stage fit and hang) now goes through per-join hooks (`AH()`). Branching keeps its own hooks, and each angled join remembers its own angle. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws and no regression. I applied its fixes: the disc and crescent no longer pop in before the strap reaches them, the tag hangs at half the turn so it stays off Jarvis's corner, the pointer starts outside the lower horn, the highlight stays on the lit side, the tag text no longer touches its moon, and the release text now says the horns draw in. An iron wing (a quarter arc chalk-marked every 10°, curled at its end) swings out of Jarvis's leg; it is drawn under the shells, so it reads as passing through a slot in GitDiscuss's leg. GitDiscuss then turns on the head to its angle with one overshoot, the screw riding the wing and sparking at each chalk mark. To set the angle, drag a leg, the wing or the tag (or GitDiscuss's nameplate), use the slider, or use ↑/↓ on the focused dividers. Tap the head (or press Enter) to release: GitDiscuss closes level, the tag comes off, the screw loosens, the wing folds away, and the dividers walk back and are lifted out. It is the felt cathedral with its join and shade, and Checks can grow (3.6 s per step growing, 2.6 s shrinking, 12 fps stop-motion). Each bay is chalked out, drops in limp and tilted, flops level and squashes. Four glass-headed pins go in, long red basting tacks run round it, and a cream running stitch follows. The basting is pulled out by its end and trails away, the pins come out, and the bay is dressed. **Aisle:** a gabled chapel bay sewn on to the left, with a pinnacle, French-knot crockets and finial, a small felt rose in the gable sewn petal by petal (the join's rose), a frieze lettered PASS RATE, and a window whose jewel pieces are sewn on round a cotton patch with 86% cross-stitched cell by cell, "passed" and "run 418 · main". **Nave:** a clerestory over Checks with a low gable, two pinnacles and a frieze lettered LAST TEN RUNS · MAIN. The ten runs are stepped lancets, taller the more passed, whose felt flips over from the backing oldest first (green all passed, amber one or two failing, red three), with a gold knot over run 418. A cotton tape below names the 2 waiting on Brent: deploy-preview · e2e-safari. Shrinking runs it backwards, so the bays are unpicked. The bays are drawn under the widgets. Before the first patch drops, anything in the way steps clear the shortest way, and the joined pair moves as one. Each placed widget is an obstacle for the next, so Jarvis can't step onto GitDiscuss. Widgets go back as the last patch lifts away, unless they were moved in the meantime. Checks is kept on the stage, and dragging or keying it keeps the grown bays on the stage too.   - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Tracery lock" join option, last in the join row (gap 22, half-gables kept). On Jarvis's end pier is an iron lock case, crested along its top. It is pierced with two cusped pointed lights and an oculus over red cloth, and it has a slot where the bolt's thumb shows the throw. A gilt escutcheon has a hinged keyhole cover. A great key with a pierced quatrefoil bow and a gilt boss hangs flat against the stone from a ring under the case. GitDiscuss's end pier has an iron staple on an arched plate. **Near:** the key sways on its ring, the cover lifts and the iron warms. **Ready:** the thrown bolt, the staple and the key in the lock are chalked in. **Joining:** the cover swings aside, and the key is lifted off and comes out toward you (it grows and its shadow drops away). As it turns to point into the lock, its shank shortens out of sight, and it is pushed home with grit. Two turns follow, each gathering speed and ending in a knock, and each drives the bolt half its throw out of the case's side, across the joint and into the staple, with grit and a spark. The red cloth behind the tracery then kindles and light leaks round the key. That light is the join light: amber and ruby while both run, cold blue if either is paused. **Release:** grab the key and turn it back anticlockwise; the key follows the hand, and the bolt draws home a half per turn. Let go before halfway and it locks again. A tap or Enter also releases it. The lock scales with any shade. Listed in the index and the page hint. The light cut off in one frame on the first drag; it now fades. The sway popped when a join started and pivoted on the bow rather than the ring. The light spilled onto the display.
