Slices are yellow felt with recessed holes, a red wax rind and a cream running stitch. **Near:** the loops fluff up and a slice hovers over the gap. **Ready:** a dashed ghost of the wedge with its slice lines. **Joining (12 fps):** the cushions close and the tabs are pressed together with a puff of fibres. Then, for each slice, GitDiscuss is lifted 6° past it, the slice is pushed in point first, and GitDiscuss is pressed down with a squash and a puff. **Angle:** −45° to +45° in 15° steps (none to three slices), set by dragging GitDiscuss's nameplate, the slider or ↑ ↓. The next slice pops in once there is nearly room, and closing squeezes the top slice out so it drops away. **Release:** tap the cheese. The slices come out last in, first out, then the Velcro rips. Engine: `bendAt` takes a join's own stepped swing (`bend`), and `peakBend` takes a join's own peak, so the vertical fit allows for the 6° lift. Joining at 0° is shorter, since only the Velcro is pressed. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, name clashes or NaN. Its must-fix is applied: at ±15° the lift went past the fit's reserved peak, so GitDiscuss could poke off the desktop. Its polish is applied too: the 0° join no longer sits idle, and its hold text is right. Left as is: tapping the cheese while a detent snap is still running can jump GitDiscuss a few degrees. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **hollow** · Angle: shell shims (Wedge), the second item of the angle round (felt's cheese wedge opened it), from the shims a mason drives under a sagging lintel in a ruined town, cut from pale shell and struck home rather than pressed
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Shell shims" join option, last in the join row (gap −6: the shells meet strap to strap; default +30°). GitDiscuss turns on a glass-capped iron pin on the joint line, at the foot when lowered (the wedge opens upward) and at the head when raised. Each shim is a 15° shard of pale shell with growth ridges, a chipped corner and a dark point where it is wedged deepest. **Near:** the strap ends glint and a shim rises out of the fog into the gap. **Ready:** a chalk ghost of the wedge with its shim lines. **Joining:** the straps meet and pins are struck home at head and foot with a spark. Then, for each shim, GitDiscuss darts 6° past where it will sit, the shim darts in point first and lodges proud, a pale crescent strike drives it home (spark, chalk ticks, grit), and GitDiscuss drops onto it with a settle. The pins' glass then kindles as the join light (amber, or cold blue-white if either is paused). **Angle:** −45° to +45° in 15° steps (none to three shims), set by dragging the shims or GitDiscuss's nameplate, the slider or ↑ ↓. The next shim darts in and is struck home once there is nearly room, and closing squeezes the top shim out to tumble into the fog. **Release:** tap the shims or press Enter. They are knocked out last in, first out by a hooked strike, GitDiscuss lifting off each and dropping onto the next, then the pins are drawn. At 0° the join is just the pins, so it is shorter. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors or undefined names, and confirmed the pivot, transform-origin, sweep flags for both signs, the stage fit and the continuity of the swing. Its must-fixes are applied: a shim tumbling out could jump to the other pin if the angle dipped through 0° (a pop now keeps its side), a pause in a drag skipped the pop animation, the hold point missed the shim at the default angle, a tap during an angle animation left the wedge off-detent, and a missing space in the hint. Its polish is applied too: there is no idle time at 0°, and the drag phase says Opening or Closing. Left as is: between detents the shims squash slightly, as the cheese does. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **gothic** · Angle: voussoirs (Wedge), the third item of the angle round (felt's cheese wedge, hollow's shell shims), from the wedge-shaped arch stones that masons hoisted on a lewis and bedded in mortar until the keystone locked the ring
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Voussoirs" join option, last in the join row (gap 0, square ends; default +30°). GitDiscuss turns about the shared corner: the bottom one when bending down (the stones rise from a carved corbel) and the top one when bending up (they hang from a small pinnacle). Each stone is a 15° wedge of dressed limestone with a drafted margin, its own mason's mark and a pierced trefoil glazed with coloured glass. The last one set is the keystone, with a gilded five-leaf boss. **Near:** the springing corners warm and a voussoir is lowered on its lewis into the gap. **Ready:** the stones are chalked out with their joints, hood and keystone. **Joining:** the springer drops onto the corner in dust. Then, for each stone, GitDiscuss swings 5° past, mortar is buttered on the bed, the stone is hauled in swaying on its rope and lodges proud, and it is struck home with dust from both joints and a glint. GitDiscuss settles onto it, the hood moulding is finished and the lewis is drawn out. The trefoils and the boss then kindle (amber and ruby, or cold blue if either is paused). **Angle:** −45° to +45° in 15° steps (none to three stones), set by dragging the stones or GitDiscuss's nameplate, the slider or the arrow keys. Opening hauls the next stone in; closing springs the top one out. **Release:** tap the boss. The stones are hauled out last in, first out, GitDiscuss lifting off each and dropping onto the next, then the springer is lifted. At 0° it is just the springer, so it is quicker. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors or undefined names and confirmed `this` binding, pivots, sweep flags and timeline order. Its must-fix is applied: the join was missing from the casing's square-end list, so the half-gables clashed when GitDiscuss bent up. Its should-fixes are applied too: a sprung-out stone now starts at its real width and, on rising wedges, flies outward and fades rather than falling across GitDiscuss. So is some polish: the hold text, the near-state stone fades when the facades overlap, and a smaller springer glow. Left as is: while dragging between detents the set stones squeeze evenly. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run.
- [x] **elven** · Angle: lily petals (Wedge), the fourth item of the angle round (felt's cheese wedge, hollow's shell shims, gothic's voussoirs): moon-white glass petals fill the opening one per 15°, so the angle reads as a water lily opening petal by petal
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Lily petals" join option, last in the join row (gap −6, so the squared ends almost touch; default +30°). GitDiscuss turns on the inside corner the casings share: the bottom one when lowered (the lily opens upward) and the top one when raised (it hangs down like a bell). **Near:** the knops at that corner glow and a petal drifts over the gap. **Ready:** the open lily is traced in dashed moonlight. **Joining:** the casings close, and at each corner a silver calyx (a bowed arm from each stem's knop, two sepals) clasps a moonstone heart. Then, for each 15°, GitDiscuss lifts 5° past, a lancet petal of moon-white glass (silver rim, midrib, two veins, warm at its base) drifts in swaying and slides in point first, and GitDiscuss settles onto it with a soft press and a glint up the petal. The petals then kindle from the heart (moonlight while both run, grey if either is paused) and a glint runs along the tips; the clasp on the open side lets go as the lily opens. **Angle:** −45° to +45° in hard 15° steps (none to three petals), via the nameplate, the slider or ↑ ↓; opening pops the next petal in, closing squeezes the top one out to float away, and a moonstone tag reads the angle and petal count. **Release:** tap the lily; the petals float away last in, first out, then the calyx lets go. At 0° the join is just the clasps, so it is shorter. Engine: optional per-join `max`, `snap` (always rest on a step) and `py` (pivot height on the casing) hooks through `amax()` and `pivY()`; the other angled joins don't set them and are unchanged. Listed in the index. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors or undefined names, and confirmed the pivot and petal geometry match GitDiscuss's CSS rotation for both signs, that `lyBend` is continuous both ways and `lyFit` is right. Its must-fix is applied: dragging through level made GitDiscuss oscillate, because the pivot changes corner there; the drag is now re-measured from the current pivot. Its should-fixes are applied too: at 0° the hit area no longer covers GitDiscuss's nameplate (it is just the two hearts), a stale petal count could pop a petal when arriving joined, and the near petal now hovers just over the gap instead of 100+ px above it. Polish too: the hold and phase text at 0°, and a settle to level no longer overshoots through 0°. Not checked in a browser or parser: node, python and Playwright needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **wire** · Angle: briolette fan (Wedge), the fifth item of the angle round (felt's cheese wedge, hollow's shell shims, gothic's voussoirs, elven's lily petals), from the wire-wrapped briolette: faceted drops wrapped onto one jump ring at the hinge corner fill the opening one per 15°
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Briolette fan" join option, last in the join row (gap 10; default +30°). Each joinable end has a wrapped eye at its inside corner (copper on Jarvis, brass on GitDiscuss), and GitDiscuss turns on a brass jump ring through both: at the foot when lowered (the fan opens upward) and at the head when raised (the stones hang like drops). Each stone is a slender faceted briolette (amethyst, citrine, aquamarine; new `br-*` gradients) with a lattice of cuts, lit and shadowed facets, and a wrap at its point: a loop round the ring, three turns down the neck, and the wire crossed into the drill. **Near:** the eyes warm and a briolette swings from an open ring on Jarvis's eye. **Ready:** the ring and the fan are dashed in. **Joining:** the pendants close, and the ring threads both eyes and twists shut with a spark. Then, for each stone, GitDiscuss lifts 5° past its place, a briolette slides in point first along its own line, the wire winds down its neck from a hot point, and GitDiscuss drops onto it. The stones then kindle (amber while either runs, cold blue if both are paused) and a glint runs out along the tips. **Angle:** −45° to +45° in hard 15° steps (none to three stones): drag the fan or GitDiscuss's nameplate, use the slider, or press ↑ ↓. Opening slides the next stone in once there is nearly room; closing squeezes the last one off the ring and it tumbles away. A slab tag reads the angle and stone count while it moves. **Release:** tap the fan or press Enter; the stones come off last in, first out, then the ring twists open. At 0° the join is just the ring. Engine: optional per-join `bend`, `py`, `peak`, `max`, `snap` and `tag` hooks on the angle object (through `pivY`, `det()`, `lim()`, `peakSwing()`, `tweenAngle` and `dialSvg`); the other four angled joins set none of them and are unchanged. Hard steps ease without overshoot, so level never swaps the pivot corner. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no must-fixes: no undefined names or clashes, no NaN, and the fan matches GitDiscuss's CSS rotation for both signs. Its should-fixes are applied: a tap during a step could release off-step (a `snapTo` target now lands it first), fast ↓↓ or slider steps were lost mid-tween (they now step from the target), the stone count had two formulas (now one `brCount`), and switching to the join while joined popped every stone in. Its polish is applied too: a squeezed stone tumbles at its own width, and stones no longer fold back on themselves at strip height. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

Then, for each course, GitDiscuss swings 5° past its place. Mortar is buttered on the bed, the voussoir (drafted margin, mason's mark, glazed trefoil) is hauled in swaying on its rope and struck home in dust, and the lewis is drawn out. Last, the trefoils kindle from the corner out and a sheen runs up the shims (amber, or cold blue if either widget is paused); at 0° the pin's glass is the light. **Angle:** −45° to +45° in hard 15° steps (none to three courses), set with the Angle slider, by dragging the wedge up or down, or with ↑ ↓; opening drives the next course in once there is nearly room, closing springs the top course out to tumble away, a parchment tag reads the angle and course count, and the pair moves to stay on the stage. **Release:** tap the springer or press Enter. Each shim is knocked out by a hooked strike and each stone hauled out, last in first out, then the pin is drawn and the springer lifted. Engine: the shared angle code now takes a per-mix face (`ang`: step, limit, `hard` easing, default angle), `fitRoom`, `angSay`, `angPh`, `angGrab` and `angMove`. Its must-fixes are applied: a rejoin mid-release snapped the courses and GitDiscuss (it now carries on from the release's position and bend), and a stale course count flung a course out on returning to the mix while joined. Its should-fixes are applied too: the angle can't change mid-build, a release stops any swing still running, and the angle is clamped to the new mix before its layout is planned. Polish applied: the tag lasts as long as the swing, and the springer stays wide enough when shaded to cover the turning corner. - [x] **gothic** · Join: windlass (Reel), a new join kind opening a Reel round (every listed item was built and the angle round ended with combined; felt had opened the last four rounds, so gothic opens this one). In a Reel join one widget casts a line, catches the other and hauls it in, so the closing itself is the animation. Built from the hoisting wheels left in cathedral roofs (Salisbury, Canterbury, Beverley)
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Windlass" join option, after Tracery lock (gap 26). On Jarvis's end pier, on an iron bracket, is an oak wheel that is also a wheel window: an iron tyre with sixteen ratchet teeth, eight spokes, eight ruby and blue panes with stone roundels at their heads, and a gilded hub. A pawl with a gilt pivot sits on the teeth. A forged hook hangs on laid hemp rope from the drum behind the wheel, and GitDiscuss's end pier has an iron ring on a plate. **Near:** the wheel rocks against its pawl, the hook sways about its eye and the iron warms. **Ready:** the taut rope, the ring and the hook seated in it are chalked across the gap. **Joining:** the pawl lifts and the wheel runs back as the hook is cast in an arc, paying out slack rope. The hook drops into the ring with a spark and the slack is taken up. Then come three heaves, each half a turn of the wheel with the pawl clicking over the teeth. Each heave drags GitDiscuss a third of the way in, with grit kicked up behind its foot, and it stands still between heaves. The rope pulls the ring round toward the wheel. The pawl drops into the teeth with a knock and a spark, and the panes kindle one by one round the wheel (amber and ruby while both run, cold blue if either is paused). **Release:** drag up on the wheel to lift the pawl (let go before halfway and it drops back), tap it, or press Enter. The light goes out, the wheel runs free and GitDiscuss rolls back, slowing; the hook is shaken off the ring and wound home, swings and settles, and the pawl drops. If Jarvis is the one dropped, the windlass hauls itself along the rope. It scales with any shade. Engine: `setJt` takes an optional per-join `close(t)` closing profile (no other join sets one, so they all still close over the first 40%). Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, undefined names, NaN or clashes, and confirmed the close profile's ends for both directions, a cancelled release drag, `pick()`, the hold at 62% and scrubbing. Its two must-fixes are applied: the rope's slack jumped at the catch and at the shake-off (it now carries on from where it was). Its should-fixes are applied too: the hook now sways about its eye instead of its seat, the chalked hook now matches the final pose, and reduced motion gets a still near pose instead of none. So is some polish: the ring wire now sits in the hook's bowl, a local `H` no longer shadows the wall height, and the hold label says "2 of 3 heaves done" between heaves. Left as is: the hook is drawn wholly in front of the ring rather than threaded through it. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright, node and python needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **felt** · Join: fishing game (Reel), the second item of the Reel round (gothic's windlass opened it), from the magnetic fishing game of felt playrooms: a toy rod casts a magnet onto a felt fish and the reel hauls it in
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Fishing game" join option, last in the join row (gap 18). A felt rod stands in a denim pocket on Jarvis's joining end: a wooden dowel with a green felt handle, a wooden cotton reel with a crank for its reel, and cream line up through a felt eye to a red felt horseshoe magnet with silver tips under a red-and-white felt float. A stuffed coral fish (yellow fins, stitched stripes, French-knot eye) with a brass ring at its mouth is sewn onto GitDiscuss's end. **Near:** the rod leans toward the fish, the magnet swings and the fish lifts its nose. **Ready:** a dashed ghost of the line and the magnet on the ring. **Joining (12 fps):** the rod is drawn back (the magnet lags out over the gap) and cast, the reel spins backwards as the line pays out, and the magnet flies across in an arc and clacks onto the ring with a spark while the fish flaps. Three turns of the reel follow: each pulls the line taut, bends the rod and hauls GitDiscuss a third of the way in while the fish wriggles, with slack between turns (the join's `moveEase` moves the pair only during the hauls). GitDiscuss lands with a squash and loose fibres, and the float's gold sequin catches the light as the join light (gold while both run, cold blue if either is paused). **Release:** tap the line, the float or the fish (or Enter): the light goes, the rod jerks the magnet off the ring, the reel winds it home, it swings and settles, and the cushions part. It all stays in the free felt at the ends, in the gap and above it, and shrinks with a shade strip. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws, no NaN, no name clashes and no pose jumps at the phase boundaries. Its must-fix is applied: during the draw-back the magnet swung onto Jarvis's nameplate, so it now lags out toward the gap and swings back on the cast. Its other fixes are applied too: a smaller wind-up keeps the reel in the free felt, the tap area now covers the fish's tail, the pop-off frame no longer snaps the magnet to its hanging place, the reel's thread no longer flips at the end of a release, the slack eases in after the pop, the ready pose holds still, and the ready text is corrected. Left as is: rejoining mid-release restarts the cast (the engine restarts any `moveEase` join from 0, as velcro does). Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright, node and python needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

- [x] **elven** · Join: silk arrow (Reel), the third item of the Reel round (gothic's windlass, felt's fishing game): a bowshot that carries a line, from the line-throwing arrow, with spider silk for the line and a spindle for the reel
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Silk arrow" join option, last in the join row (gap 60). A silver crescent bow is bracketed to Jarvis's knops, with recurved horns curling into beaded spirals and a moonstone grip between two leaves. Behind the grip is a moonstone spindle of spider silk, and the silk is tied onto a leaf-bladed arrow with leaf fletchings and a moonstone nock. A night-glass lancet target on two arms (a strike ring, a moonstone above it and a leaf below) stands on GitDiscuss's stem. **Near:** the bow is drawn a little and the grip and the target's moonstone glow. **Ready:** the silk and the arrow in the target are traced in dashed moonlight. **Joining:** the bow is drawn and loosed, and the string shivers. The spindle spins back as the silk pays out, and the arrow flies a low arc, turning along it, sinks into the ring with a glint and quivers. Three turns of the spindle each take up the slack and then haul GitDiscuss a third of the way in while the bow leans; GitDiscuss stands still between turns and the arrow levels. Then the silk kindles with three dew beads as the join light (moonlight while both run, grey if either is paused), and a glint runs along it. **Release:** tap the arrow or the silk, or press Enter. The silk dims, the spindle tugs the arrow free, winds it back tail first onto the string, and the casings part. Everything scales with a shade strip. Engine: `setJt` takes an optional per-join `close(t)` for joining only (no other elven join sets one), and `doJoin` restarts such a join from 0 when rejoined mid-release. Listed in the index. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, undefined names, clashes, NaN or bad phase hand-offs. Its must-fix is applied: rejoining mid-release made the pair jump about 110 px. Its should-fixes are applied too: the spindle no longer snaps half a turn at the end of a release, the strike spark grows from nothing, the arrow and silk glows use the wider `sgSoft` filter, the gap went from 44 to 60 so the struck arrow clears the grip and spindle, and the brackets reach the knops.   - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Grapnel" join option, last in the join row (gap 44). Every earlier container (stone niche, felt pocket, wire hoop, swan boat) has the other widget set down in it; this one fetches what it holds, the way a slipway's winch hauls a boat up onto its cradle
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Hauled up the slip" mix, last in the picker, reusing the needle mix's `hollowfelt` styles (Hollow Jarvis with a plain riveted strap, felt GitDiscuss and Checks). Gap 30. At rest an iron winch stands on Jarvis's back by the strap: an A-frame, a drum of pale silk with a toothed flange, a pawl, a crank with a bone knob and a glass hub. Its line runs over a brass sheave on the strap's spike to an iron hook barbed with shell, hanging in the gap. GitDiscuss has a cream cord loop on a teal felt tab at its top-left corner. **Near:** the hook leans toward GitDiscuss, the first chitin plate peeks out from under Jarvis's foot, and the loop stands up and glows. **Ready:** the cradle, its two thorns and the taut line into the loop are chalked in. **Joining:** five chitin plates telescope out from under Jarvis's foot one after another, each clicking out with a chalk tick, and run under GitDiscuss's foot. A thorn stands on the first plate in the gap and another lies flat at the far end. The pawl lifts and the hook is cast in a high arc, tumbling, while the drum pays out; it drops into the loop with a spark and the line falls slack. Then come three heaves (a per-mix `travel` curve): each takes up the slack, yanks GitDiscuss a third of the way along the cradle while the felt squashes and fibres drag off its foot, and pauses. The far thorn then flicks up behind it with a spark, and the winch's glass hub kindles as the join light (amber, or cold blue-white if either is paused), with a glint along the cradle's lip. The drum's turn is worked out from the length of line out, so it spins back on the cast and winds in on each heave. The cradle runs under the cushion's foot and the thorns stand off its ends, so the display, tabs and buttons are never covered. **Release:** tap the winch, pull the line or press Enter. The light goes out, the thorn folds flat, the hook is shaken free and wound home, GitDiscuss slides off, and the plates go back in, outermost first. A rejoin partway through plays that backwards. Engine: an optional per-mix `readyPull` (this mix uses .4), so a drop at Ready still leaves the heaves something to haul. Listed in the index. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws, no NaN, no name clashes and no pose jumps. Its must-fixes are applied: the Ready ghost and the flat far thorn were drawn over the widgets and anchored to Jarvis, so they could cross GitDiscuss's tabs and buttons when it stood lower. They are now drawn behind the widgets, and the far thorn comes in front only as it flicks up. Its should-fixes are applied too: the plate ticks are behind the widgets, the glint is lower, the winch stays seated on the shell when shaded, and the loop's glow fades on a ready drop. So is its polish: ghost opacity, the loop holding up through a held join, a "Rejoining" phase label, `??` for `readyPull`, plate 0's tick timing, and "tumbling" for the cast. Left as is: a drop at Ready from inside the joined position heaves GitDiscuss a few pixels outward, and the status line on a mid-release rejoin reads as a fresh join. Not checked in a browser or parser: node and python needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

Angles had the fewest items (five per page), and gothic had waited longest to open a round. In a Stay the angle is held by a brace across the opening, not by a pivot's friction (Hinge) or by what fills the wedge (Wedge). This one comes from the wrought-iron casement stay: a bar pivoted on the window, whose holes drop over a pin on the frame
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Casement stay" join option, last in the join row (gap 0, square ends, −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°). GitDiscuss turns about the corner it shares with Jarvis, as on the fan vault. A black iron stay runs from a bracket on GitDiscuss's end to a gilt-headed pin on Jarvis's: over the roofline when bending down, under the foot when bending up. The stay has a twisted neck, a swell round each hole with the smith's dots counting the holes, a fishtail end, and a gilt quatrefoil on the pivot. The pivot-to-pin distance is 2r·sin(φ+θ/2), so each of the four holes lands exactly on the pin at its 15° step. **Near:** the stone warms where the brackets will stand. **Ready:** the brackets, bar, holes and the casement's swing are chalked out. **Joining:** the brackets are set with grit, and the stay is dropped onto its pivot, held raised over the roof (or hanging under the foot), then brought onto the pin. It lands on hole 1 with a clank and a glint. Then it is lifted, GitDiscuss swings 5° past its angle and settles, and the stay drops onto its hole. The quatrefoil's glass kindles as the join light (amber while both run, cold blue if paused). **Angle:** drag the stay or the nameplate, or use the slider or arrow keys. The stay rides lifted over the pin with its holes sliding past and the shadow widening, and drops onto the nearest hole with a clank when the swing stops. A parchment label reads "on hole n of 4" or "stay lifted". **Release:** tap the pin. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors and confirmed the geometry at every step in both directions. Its must-fixes are applied. When bending down the stay is now raised over the roof rather than swept across both facades, and the range makes room for it. A 0° release no longer stalls for half a second. Its should-fixes are applied too: a clank on every drop mid-join and mid-release, the pin and pivot riding their brackets as they rise, a missing space in the hint, a release phase label, and the unused `state()` removed. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors or name clashes, and confirmed the eyelet registration for both signs and about 3 px of clearance over the cushion corners at ±45° and the overshoot. Its must-fix is applied: a join released from Ready no longer drops the tongue and snaps the strap back for a frame. Its should-fixes are applied too. - [x] **combined** · Pairwise seam for the Stay kind: silvered bone stay (Model D, after the second angle round). Every listed item was built and combined was next after the round; Model D had waited longest among the combined models (its last turn was pinned voussoirs), and elven + Hollow was the one pair never given an authored seam
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Silvered bone stay" mix, last in the picker (pair `elfhollow`: elven Jarvis, Hollow GitDiscuss and Checks; gap 0; −45° to +45° in hard 15° steps, default +30°). GitDiscuss turns about the shared corner, as in pinned voussoirs: the stay runs over the roofs when it is lowered and under the bellies when it is raised. Hollow brings a waisted rib bone with a double-knobbed end, pivoted on an iron post (scrolled knee brace, riveted plate) on GitDiscuss's strap with a brass rivet bound in red silk. Elven brings a moonstone pin on a silver stalk from Jarvis's stem, with a tendril and a leaf. During the join a silver vine runs onto the bone from the pin and along it both ways, wrapping it (back half faint, front half bright), and opens four leaf-shaped eyes rimmed in silver, with leaves between them. Each eye sits at the pin-to-pivot distance for its step, recomputed at the strip height, so at every step the pin is exactly in an eye. **Near:** a moonstone glimmers over Jarvis's stem and the strap cap glints. **Ready:** brackets, bone, eyes and the swing are traced in dashed moonlight. **Joining:** the brackets rise and grow with grit, the bone is set and lowered to hover, the silver opens the eyes, the bone drops onto eye 1 with a glint and a ring of moonlight, lifts, GitDiscuss swings 5° past and settles, the bone drops onto its eye, and the moonstone kindles (moonlight while both run, grey if either is paused) with a glint along the vine. **Angle:** drag the stay, use the slider or ↑ ↓. The bone lifts off the pin while GitDiscuss turns and drops onto the nearest eye when it stops; crossing level, it fades from one side to the other. A night-glass tag reads "on eye n of 4" or "stay lifted". **Release:** tap the pin (or Enter). Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) confirmed the syntax, names, rotation sense, eye registration and clearances at ±45° (full and shaded), and that the lift loop ends. Its must-fixes are applied: the clink on the final eye never showed, and a late rejoin could leave the pin dim while joined (the blend window now stops at 1). Its should-fixes are applied too: the bone's swing-up while being set, and the lift at 0°, are capped so the long end stays inside the kept room, and at level the side follows the set angle. So is its polish: the swing starts after the bone lifts, rejoin blends never close the eyes again, side-change wording, and two phase labels. Left as is: the angle can still be changed while held mid-join with the scrubber, as in pinned voussoirs. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright, node and osascript needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **wire** · Join: tube rivets (Rivet), a new join kind opening a Rivet round. Every listed item was built and the second angle round ended with combined. Joins had last had a round five rounds ago (Reel), and wire had never opened a join round and had waited longest of the skins that hadn't. In a Rivet the casings overlap and are pinned through, unlike any earlier kind (Hinge, Interlock, Bind, Grow, Bridge, Clasp, Fuse, Swarm, Lock, Reel). This one comes from the jeweller's cold connection: a brass tube through drilled holes, flared with a chasing hammer
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Tube rivets" join option, last in the join row (gap 40). Jarvis carries a hammered copper tab with a scalloped end, wrapped to its frame and stamped along both edges. GitDiscuss carries a three-lobed brass tab edged in twisted wire. Each tab has three drilled holes. **Near:** the holes run hot and the brass tab lifts off its frame. **Ready:** a dashed ghost of the brass tab over the copper one, with the rivets marked. **Joining:** the brass slides over the copper, and the holes line up with a glint. Three tube rivets drop in (top, bottom, then middle through an amber rondelle), each landing with a clink. A chasing hammer (brass head, ball peen, wire-wrapped handle, jet knob) taps each twice, and each blow throws a spark and splits the tube wider into a six-petal rosette. Then the rondelle kindles as the join light (amber, labradorite if either is paused) and a glint runs down the lobes. It all scales with shade, with the rosettes kept a little larger on a strip. **Release:** tap the rivets or press Enter. The heads pop off one by one, middle first, from whatever state the join had reached, then the tabs part. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors or name clashes and confirmed the lobe arcs, the hammer orientation, the timings and that nothing reaches the displays. Its must-fixes are applied. A reduced-motion release (or Hold then Separate) no longer leaves rivets drawn after the pair parts. The hammer's spark is now timed on the blow so it actually shows. So are its should-fixes. The hammer scales with shade. A rivet released mid-drop pops from where it was. Rejoining mid-release drops the rivets in afresh. It also suggested polish, and four items were applied: the rondelle colour follows the box clasp, the hammer's shadow falls down-right, the last two blows no longer overlap, and the hit area is centred. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run.
- [x] **gothic** · Join: hot rivets (Rivet), the second item of the Rivet round (wire's tube rivets opened it; gothic had waited longest, its last item being the second angle round's casement stay), from medieval hot riveting: a rivet heated in the forge is hammered over while soft, and shrinks as it cools to draw two lapped bars tight
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Hot rivets" join option, last in the join row (gap 30, half-gables kept). Each end pier keeps a wrought-iron strap in a keeper with only its end showing. Jarvis's is wide, with punched edges and a cusped trefoil end. GitDiscuss's is narrow and ends in a spear with two curled lobes. **Near:** the strap ends stir out of their keepers and glow red. **Ready:** both straps, lapped, and the three rivets are chalked across the gap. **Joining:** the straps run out and lap, GitDiscuss's over Jarvis's, and a glint runs along the holes as they line up. Three rivets go in one at a time: Jarvis's pier, GitDiscuss's pier, then the middle one over the gap. Each comes in toward the wall yellow-hot, shrinking as it lands with grit. A smith's hammer (oak handle, iron wedge, cross-peen head) swings down on it twice from a hand kept in the gap, so it never crosses a window. Each blow throws sparks and spreads the head, the second dresses it into a faceted rose, and the head cools through orange and cherry red to black. Then a gilt quatrefoil cap is struck onto the middle rivet, and its glass boss kindles as the join light (amber while both run, cold blue if either is paused). It all scales down with any shade. **Release:** tap the rivets or press Enter. The light goes out and the cap comes off, the hammer knocks each head off spinning with a spark, middle first, and the straps draw back before the facades part. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found balanced brackets, escaped strings and no undefined names. It confirmed that the joined pose matches in both directions, the hammer lifts the right way (R2 mirrored too), the timing windows don't overlap, and nothing reaches the displays or nameplates. Its must-fix is applied: the last frame of every release threw, because the release branch read the timing of the popped heads at jt 0 while the direction was still −1. That left the overlay frozen and the release unfinished. Its should-fix is applied too: the straps jumped back about 7 px when a join started from Ready. So is some of its polish: "white-hot" in the hold text, and the cap phase label now starts with the cap. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Bone trenails" join option, last in the join row (gap 30). Jarvis's strap keeps a dark iron flange slid away inside it, and GitDiscuss's keeps a pale shell scale, scalloped in three lobes like the belly plates, with growth lines and three bored holes. Only their lips show. **Near:** the lips edge out and glint. **Ready:** a chalk ghost of the lapped plates with the three pegs marked. **Joining:** the shells close, the flange and scale slide out, the scale laps over the iron with its shadow on it, and a glint runs down the holes as they line up. Three bone trenails go in, top, bottom, then middle. Each drops in point first and stands proud of its hole (drawn larger and up-left, toward you). A stone maul on a bone haft lashed with red silk winds up toward you (it grows and its shadow falls further off), comes down on the proud head and drives it home with a spark, chalk ticks and grit off the plates. A small wedge is set in the peg's end grain and a second blow drives it, so the head splits and spreads. The maul carries across lifted between pegs. The middle wedge is a sliver of glass, and it kindles as the join light (amber while both run, cold blue-white if either is paused). Everything scales with any shade. **Release:** tap the plates or press Enter. The glass goes dark, the maul knocks each wedge out with a glancing blow, middle first, the peg springs proud and spins away into the fog, and the plates slide home before the shells part. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found balanced brackets, escaped strings and no undefined names, NaN or name clashes. It confirmed the peg orders (in 0,2,1; out 1,0,2), that the holes line up at mid-gap when joined, that the maul face lands exactly on its target, and that the maul stays clear of both displays at full swing. Its must-fix is applied: the maul snapped from raised straight onto the peg at the start of each wind-up (it now holds up with a small wind-up overshoot). Its should-fix is applied too: the maul jumped about 10 px when it moved to the next peg (it now heads for the proud head). So is some of its polish: the lips no longer pull in before sliding out on a join from Ready, the hold text matches the held frame, thrown pegs spin about their heads, and the plates are home before the shells start parting. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

Jarvis's is silver with three round lobes and a hairline vine engraved on it. GitDiscuss's is frosted moonstone glass with three pointed lobes, silver-rimmed eyes and fine veins. **Near:** the plates peek out of the stems and their lobe tips glimmer. **Ready:** the lapped plates and three flowers are traced in dashed moonlight. **Joining:** the casings close, the plates unfurl and the glass laps over the silver with its shadow on it, and a glint runs down the eyes as they line up. Then the rivets go in one at a time, top, bottom, then middle. - [x] **combined** · Containment for the Rivet kind: riveted setting (Model E, after the Rivet round). Every listed item was built and combined was next after the Rivet round; Model E had waited longest among the combined models (its last turn was the Reel round's slip). Every Rivet join pins two overlapping casings through each other; this asks what a rivet does when one widget holds another. The answer comes from the jeweller's cold connection: a found shell is never drilled, it is held in a setting and only the setting is riveted, so in containment the rivets belong to the container
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Riveted setting" mix, last in the picker (pair `hoophollow`: wire Jarvis, Hollow GitDiscuss with its plain riveted strap toward the hinge, Hollow Checks; gap 36). Jarvis's frame end carries a three-knuckle hinge (two rolled copper knuckles and a coil band round the pin), with its plate folded out of sight. **Near:** the plate swings ajar and its rim, holes and knuckles run hot. **Ready:** the open setting, its seven folded tabs and rivets are traced in dashed copper behind the widgets. **Joining:** a hammered, patinated copper plate (stamped dot border, a cabochon in a wire bezel on its neck) swings out flat about the hinge with a springy overshoot. GitDiscuss slides onto it and settles with a squash. Seven tabs cut from the plate's edge fold up and over the shell's rim one by one, clockwise from the top left. Each is foreshortened along the fold, a bright sliver edge on, and flashes the raw copper of its back as it lands with a glint and a rolled lip at the bend. Seven brass tube rivets then drop through the folded tabs into the plate's holes with a clink. A chasing hammer flares each into a six-petal rosette with one blow, turning over in the hand to reach the belly's rivets from below. The cabochon kindles as the join light (amber while both run, lapis if either is paused) and a glint runs round the rivets. Tabs reach only a few pixels over the rim, and the top tips stop at y 8, so the nameplate, display, tabs and buttons stay clear, full and shaded. **Release:** tap or pull the copper margin (an evenodd ring round the shell), or press Enter. The light goes out, the rivet heads pop off outward, last first, the tabs unfold, the shell slides out and the plate folds away. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors or name clashes, and confirmed the dorsal curve, clearances, layer split and pose continuity. Its must-fix is applied: the focus ring covered the whole tray with `pointer-events:all`, so once joined a click on GitDiscuss's tabs or buttons released the pair; the ring now has `pointer-events="none"`. Its should-fixes are applied: the plate no longer shuts for one frame after a Ready drop, the hammer's offset is continuous as it turns over, and the hold caption matches jt .66. Most of its polish is applied too: popped heads fly outward, not over the shell; the top tips are clamped short of the nameplate; the room is enlarged for the swing's overshoot and the hammer; and a missing space in the `st-lapis` gradient tag is fixed. Left as is: on a rejoin mid-release the hammer appears without fading in. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright, node and awk needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Font cover" shade option, last in the shade row (64 px strip, 54 for Checks; 3.6 s). The display fades and the bay darkens into a niche. A cover in three stages is let down out of the frieze on its chain, gathering speed until it lands on the sill and rebounds once. Each stage is narrower than the one below and is a capped oak box with blind arches painted red, blue and green, with gilt edges and bosses. Gilt cresting runs along each ledge. The top stage carries spirelets and a gilt finial set with a glass orb. As the wall lowers, the foot rides up while the finial stays hung, so lowering the wall is what nests the stages. The top stage slides down inside the middle one, then the middle one into the base, with dust off each ledge as it seats. `nsGeo` sizes each stage's rim so the nested rims exactly fill the strip. The value is then gilded onto a black cartouche on the base, and the orb kindles as the status: amber running, blue paused, red to decide, green passed. Nothing moves at rest, unshading plays it back, and joins still work. Listed in the index, and the "Plain lower" text lists it. The reviewer (reading only) confirmed the stage heights fill the strip exactly at both sizes, the draw order, the clipping and the phase continuity. The value text is 1.5 px smaller so its descenders no longer clip on the cartouche, the gilding clip is no longer cut at the bottom, the orb's glow is drawn outside the clip so it isn't cut flat at the frieze, a dead condition is gone, and the cresting starts clear of the cap above. A cotton label pops on with the name satin-stitched and the value cross-stitched. **The middle spool's thread is the status** (amber running, blue paused, red needs you, green passed). Nothing moves at rest. Unshading unpicks the label, lifts the trays up and in (left first, each with a backOut overshoot), puts the goods and trays away, sinks the box and refills the cushion with a bounce. The page has no foot anchoring, and with the top anchored the box would have risen to meet its trays. So the engine gained gothic's foot anchoring (a per-shade `foot` flag): `shiftFeet` and `footOff` are hooked into toggleShade's frames and into `pick` for switching shades while shaded. `pairBox` now measures at the current height under a foot shade, and `lowH` follows the live height. Listed in the index and the page hint, and the "Plain deflate" text lists it. My first helper names (`sb*`) clashed with the buckled strap's `sbDraw`/`sbGeo`, so they are `sew*`. The reviewer (reading only) found no must-fixes: no clashes, no syntax errors, the shift sign is correct, there are no pops at phase boundaries, there are no tray collisions in either order, the goods stay inside the box at both sizes, and the label text fits. Its should-fixes are applied. `pairBox` used the full height, so the pair could rise for no reason. The thimble was hidden between the open trays, so it and the pincushion moved outward. The opening overshoot was about 5e-7, so it is now a plain backOut. Two polish items are applied too: the arms fade in after their tray starts to grow, and `lowH` is fixed for foot shades. Left as is (gothic and hollow behave the same): a shaded strip dragged to the stage's top edge keeps its top there when unshaded, so its foot moves down. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright, node, python and perl needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

Then the widget closes to 62 px (45 for Checks), and top first each gap's stitches are snipped (the gold ends fall and fade) and the turn slumps inside the one below. The name is satin-stitched onto the cotton, then the value in the status colour, and a wool ball is sewn to the inner end as the status (amber running, raspberry to decide, green passed, blue-grey paused).   - Added to `combined.html` as the "One fan" mix, last in the picker, with Turned to stone's widgets (a stone group with an oriel bay, felt Checks) and its join and shade. The tips are blanket-stitched, a tick is sewn on each passed one, a red pin goes into each waiting one, and a pinked cotton tape unrolls with "12 / 14 checks passed · run 418 · main · 2 waiting". **Docked**, the felt fan folds as Checks is drawn into the bay. The ripple turns Checks to stone and runs on into the bay's foot, which opens smoothly into a fan-vaulted corbel in place of the corbelled courses. A band slides out of the sill with dust, the pendant drops on the centre rib, and fourteen ribs swing out with shaded panels. Ring ribs and blind cusped lights are carved in, and a glass roundel per check kindles from the middle outward: plum (Checks' kept colour) for passed, amber for waiting. The count is cut into the band letter by letter, then a glint crosses the vault. The ring pull hangs from the vault's pendant. Pulling it drops the sill with the vault on it, so the ring follows the pointer. Once let go, the vault folds back into the sill while the shell cracks, and the felt fan opens again as Checks leaves. The group rises and Checks steps clear (and back) to make room; an undocked fanned Checks goes where its fan fits; drags keep the fan on the stage. Engine changes: the oriel's foot is now `orFoot`/`orPendant` (Turned to stone unchanged) with an optional per-mix `foot`; there are also an optional `spot` hook on `sepSpot`, an optional `placed` hook after the under layer is written (so dragging a fanned Checks moves the fan without rebuilding it), and `mbE` reads a mix's `cExt`. Second, an undocked fanned Checks could put its fan off the stage and over the bay. Its should-fixes are applied too: the copy now matches the timeline, the felt fan folds sooner and is drawn behind the stone, and dragging no longer rebuilds the felt filter. - [x] **hollow** · Angle: cog roll (Roll), a new angle kind opening a third angle round. Every listed item was built and the Fan morph round ended with combined; the order of rounds runs angle after morph, and hollow had opened the fewest rounds. In a Roll the joint has no fixed pivot: GitDiscuss rolls round a cog on Jarvis's end like a planet gear on its carrier, so its pivot travels (unlike Hinge, Wedge or Stay). Built from the gear trains of an old clockwork town
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Cog roll" join option, last in the join row (gap 64, −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°). Two equal twelve-tooth iron cogs (pierced, a scored ring, a cold catch-light) fold out of the straps' middle eyelets. **Near:** the eyelets glint and small cogs peep out, turning. **Ready:** a chalk ghost of the meshed cogs, the notched sector and needle, the carrier and the arc GitDiscuss's hub will travel. **Joining:** the shells close. Jarvis's cog swings out on a telescoping arm, spinning, then GitDiscuss's follows, and they mesh with a clank. A notched iron sector rises behind Jarvis's cog, and an iron carrier with a pale bone needle is dropped onto both hubs, its brass caps struck home with sparks. GitDiscuss then rolls round Jarvis's cog to its angle, a little past and back. Its cog turns tooth over tooth and its hub travels round on the carrier, so it turns twice as far as the carrier swings: θ = 2α, as a CSS transform `rotate(α) translate(2R) rotate(α) translate(−2R)` about Jarvis's hub, which the overlay's `roll()` matches. The needle crosses seven chalked notches with a spark at each, and the glass in Jarvis's hub cap kindles as the join light (amber, or cold blue-white if either is paused). **Angle:** drag the cogs or carrier (counted double, since they swing half as far), drag GitDiscuss's nameplate, use the slider, or press ↑ ↓; each notch crossed clicks and it settles on the nearest. The mechanism shrinks to 0.72 scale in a shade strip. **Release:** tap the glass hub (or Enter). GitDiscuss rolls back level, the carrier is lifted off, the sector sinks and the cogs fold away. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax, runtime or NaN problems and no must-fixes. It confirmed the roll matches the CSS transform, the teeth mesh at every angle, and everything clears Jarvis's strap and shell at ±50° and in shade. Its should-fixes are applied: a drop at Ready no longer hides the peeping cogs while the shells close, the sector is narrowed so GitDiscuss's teeth no longer graze it at ±45°, the resting-notch spark no longer freezes, and a 0° release no longer stalls. So is its polish: the caps arrive with the carrier, the arriving cog finishes its spin before meshing, and the hub has a pointer cursor. Left as is: rejoining mid-release snaps (shared engine, as on the bone stay). Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

Elven things are bound, not geared, so the roll comes from the rolling joint of the Jacob's ladder toy: two equal discs held by two ribbons that cross between them, each clasped to both, so they can only roll on each other
  - Added to `elven.html` as the "Ribbon roll" join option, last in the join row (gap 72, −45° to +45° in hard 15° steps, default +30°). Two silver roundels (a night-glass face with six openwork silver leaves round a moonstone hub) stand in the gap on curling arms from the stems. One silk ribbon is moon-white and the other sea-green, each clasped at both ends by a small silver leaf. When GitDiscuss's hub has travelled α round Jarvis's, GitDiscuss has turned θ = 2α. That motion is one rotation by θ about a point R·tan(θ/4) off the gap's middle, so the engine gained an optional `oy(bd,hh)` hook (through a new `pivOY`, used by render() and pairBox()) that moves GitDiscuss's transform origin with the bend; the other angled joins don't set it and are unchanged. The ribbons are arcs computed each frame (moon-white from Jarvis's top through the contact and under GitDiscuss's, sea-green the other way), so one winds off Jarvis's roundel onto GitDiscuss's while the other winds back, each keeping 180° of length. **Near:** the stems glimmer and small roundels peep out. **Ready:** the roundels, crossed ribbons and the arc GitDiscuss's hub will travel are traced in dashed moonlight. **Joining:** the arms curl out, the roundels unfurl with a turn, the moon-white ribbon is wound on and clasped with a glint, then the sea-green one across it; GitDiscuss rolls to its angle, a little past and back, the moonstone on its rim showing it turn; Jarvis's hub and the contact kindle as the join light (moonlight running, grey paused) and a glint runs along both ribbons. **Angle:** drag GitDiscuss's nameplate, the slider or ↑ ↓; each 15° stop passed glints at the contact, and a moonstone tag reads the angle and its star (one of seven). **Release:** tap the roundels (or Enter); the ribbons are unwound last first and the roundels furl before the casings part. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) verified the rotation centre algebraically, that the CSS origin and the overlay's `roll()` agree, that both ribbons are continuous and constant in length, the clearances at ±50° full and shaded, the stage fit, syntax and escaping. Its must-fix is applied: the join lacked `part:.3`, so on release the casings parted while the roundels were still drawn at the joined place. Its should-fix is applied too: the stop glint fired halfway between stops (it now uses `Math.floor`). So is some polish: a phase boundary and a hit circle 1 px past GitDiscuss's end. Left as is: switching joins while joined and back can fire one stray glint. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

Hollow's and elven's rolls are two equal discs rolling on each other; this one rolls a half disc along a straight track, like the rocker of a rolling lift bridge, so GitDiscuss slides as it tips. Built from the tympanum, the half-round carved panel over a church door, set on its jambs
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Tympanum roll" join option, last in the join row (gap 60, square ends, −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°). GitDiscuss's joining end carries a tympanum on its side. It has a voussoir archivolt studded with seven gilt ball-flowers, six cusped glass lights, and a gilt quatrefoil hub at mid-wall. Jarvis's end carries a coursed door jamb with a roll moulding, a pinnacle and seven round sockets. The rim bears on the jamb's face, so turning by θ moves the hub R·θ along it. That is a CSS `translateY(Rθ) rotate(θ)` about the hub, which the overlay matches. Down is bending down and up is bending up, and one ball-flower sits in one socket every 15°. **Near:** Jarvis's end and the hub seat warm. **Ready:** the jamb, the tympanum at its angle and the hub's path (a tick per socket) are chalked out. **Joining:** the facades close, the jamb is built up from the foot with grit, and the tympanum swings out of GitDiscuss's end like a door leaf. Its middle ball-flower drops into the middle socket with a knock and dust. Then GitDiscuss rolls to its angle, a little past and back, each socket knocking with a glint. The glass then kindles light by light as the join light (amber and ruby while both run, cold blue if either is paused). **Angle:** drag the tympanum up or down the jamb (pointer travel = hub travel), swing GitDiscuss's nameplate (pivoting about the moving hub), use the slider or press ↑ ↓. A parchment label reads the angle and the socket. Shaded, the tympanum and jamb shrink to 0.7, not to the strip, so the rim always clears the jamb, and the jamb stands proud of the strip. **Release:** tap the gilt hub (or Enter). The glass goes dark, it rolls level, the tympanum swings in and the jamb sinks. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, undefined names or NaN. It confirmed that the CSS matches the overlay, the rim stays tangent to the jamb, the sockets line up and the stage fits. Its should-fixes are applied. The other rolls put a disc on a disc or on a straight track; this one rolls a wheel *inside* a hoop twice its size, like a bangle turning in a bigger bangle, which makes a point on the rim run in a straight line (the Tusi couple)
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Cradle roll" join option, last in the join row (gap 56, −45° to +45° in hard 15° steps, default +30°). Jarvis's end carries a cradle on three bound stubs: a section of copper hoop (two wires bound with coil wraps, scrolled ends), seven gold seeds on its back for the stops, and a fine brass bar across its middle. GitDiscuss's end carries a wheel of twisted brass wire, half the cradle's size, on a forked bail, with six fine spokes and a stone hub. A pearl fixed on the wheel's rim is threaded on the bar. Rolling inside a hoop twice its size, the hub goes as far round the cradle as GitDiscuss turns, so GitDiscuss's transform is `translate(r(1−cos θ), r·sin θ) rotate(θ)` about the hub. It sinks and swings out a little as it bends down, and the pearl runs straight along the bar, further the more it tips either way. The engine gained an optional `tr(th,h)` angle hook (in render() and pivotSpan()) for a turning point that travels; the other angled joins don't set it. **Near:** both ends warm. **Ready:** a dashed ghost of the cradle, the wheel at the set angle, the bar, and the hub's arc with a tick per stop. **Joining:** the stubs and hoop are drawn out of Jarvis's frame and bound, the bail, ring, spokes and hub out of GitDiscuss's, and the wheel seats with a clink. The bar slides across and the pearl drops onto it, then GitDiscuss rolls to its angle, 3° past and back, each stop glinting at the contact as it passes. The hub kindles as the join light (amber, labradorite if both are paused), the stop under the wheel lights, and a glint runs round the rim. **Angle:** drag the wheel round the cradle (it follows the pointer about the cradle's centre), drag GitDiscuss's nameplate, use the slider or press ↑ ↓; a tag reads the angle and "on stop n of 7". **Release:** tap the wheel or press Enter; the timeline plays back. Shaded, cradle and wheel scale to 0.6, and the hoop stands a little proud of the strip. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, TDZ problems, name clashes or NaN. It confirmed that the CSS matches the overlay, the rim stays tangent, the pearl stays on its bar, the drag sign is right, and the casings clear the cradle at ±48°, full and shaded. Its should-fix is applied: the hoop's scroll ends were drawn at the full hoop's ends while it was still growing, so they floated ahead of the wire. Two polish items are applied too: the middle stop seed no longer sits on Jarvis's frame, and the coil cache is renamed. Left as is: the angle tag can overlap Jarvis's foot when the pair is low on the stage, as on the other wire tags.   - Added to `felt.html` as the "Log rocker" join option, last in the join row (gap 43, −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°). A felt log slice (brown bark with stitched ridges, a cream face with two running-stitch growth rings and a split, a sequin heart) stands at Jarvis's end on a felt axle strap, behind the end's edge. GitDiscuss's end carries a felt plank with stitched grain and seven ticks, R·15° apart. A straight edge rolling on a circle: turned θ, GitDiscuss is translated by R(cosθ−1+θ·sinθ, sinθ−θ·cosθ) and turned θ about the plank's middle. So it slides and sinks a little as it tips, its pivot travels, and the tick touching the log reads the angle. The angle frame gained an optional `sy` (vertical shift) in `hingePt` and the CSS transform; no other join sets it. **Near:** the log peeks out from behind Jarvis's end and rocks. **Ready:** a dashed ghost of the log and of the plank at the set angle. **Joining (12 fps):** the cushions close while the log rolls out (turning by the distance it travels), the plank lands on it with a squash and fibres, and GitDiscuss rocks to its angle, a little past and back. A gold French knot lights on the resting tick with a glint, and the sequin glows as the join light (gold running, cold blue if either is paused). **Angle:** nameplate drag, slider or ↑ ↓; each tick's knot lights as it touches the log, and a cross-stitch tag shows the angle. **Release:** tap the log (or Enter). The log shrinks in a strip so the ±45° contact stays on the plank's flat face. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) confirmed the rolling transform, that the CSS, `hingePt` and the overlay agree, the clearances at ±49° at full and strip height (the nearest corner is 4.7 px clear of Jarvis's end), and pose continuity both ways, including from Ready. Its must-fix is applied: in a strip the ±45° tick and knot fell off the plank, so the log's radius now keeps the contact clear of the plank's rounded ends. Its should-fixes are applied too: the landing squash is now about the contact point, so it presses rather than opening a gap, and the axle strap fades in instead of popping. So are the phase labels. Left as is: `model.fast` can survive an interrupted release into a rejoin (shared with the strap and fan), and the angle drag measures about mid-gap rather than the log. Every listed item was built and combined was next after the Roll round, which no combined item had closed yet; Model A had waited longest among the combined models (its last turn was storeys, buttoned). This asks what a standard face is for a roll. The answer comes from gearing: the face is a pitch, as gear-makers cut to a module so that any two gears mesh. Wire and Hollow had never met at a standard face
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Bead pinion, iron rack" mix, last in the picker (pair `hoophollow`: wire Jarvis, Hollow GitDiscuss and Checks; gap 48; −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°). The face is a pitch circle (R 26, 15 in a strip), a straight pitch line and a tooth every 15°. **Wire** can't cut teeth, so it strings them: 24 beads threaded on a copper hoop at the pitch (amber round a garnet on the working arc, pearl and lapis behind), six spiral spokes and a cabochon hub, on a twisted stem bound to Jarvis's end. **Hollow** forges an iron rack riveted over GitDiscuss's strap, notched at the same pitch, with chalk ticks by the seven working notches. GitDiscuss rolls with no fixed pivot: it moves by R(cosθ−1+θ·sinθ, sinθ−θ·cosθ) and turns about the rack's middle, so a bead sits in a notch at every step. The engine's `turn` hook can now return a translation too (other mixes are unchanged). **Apart:** the pinion lies folded flat against Jarvis's end. **Near:** it swings ajar and the rack glints. **Ready:** the pinion, the rack's pitch line at the set angle and the far end's rolling path are traced in dashed lamplight. **Joining (smooth):** the pinion swings and spins open, a little past and back. The notches close over the beads with a knock, a ring and grit, and GitDiscuss rocks once as they seat, then rolls to its angle 4° past and back. Each bead glows as it takes the weight, and the tick by the notch in use burns amber. The hub kindles as the join light (amber, or lapis if either is paused) with a glint round the hoop. **Angle:** drag the rack, use the slider or press ↑ ↓; a tag counts the teeth rolled. **Release:** tap the hub (or Enter). The pitch and the notches are recut for the strip. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found nothing that throws and confirmed that the CSS and SVG transforms agree, the rolling maths, the clearance at ±49° (6.8 px from Jarvis at full height), pose continuity and that other mixes are untouched. Its must-fixes are applied: in a strip the ±45° notches were not cut (the strip pitch radius is now smaller, so they fit with margin), and a separate after a mid-release rejoin left GitDiscuss tilted while apart (`bpBend` now returns 0 at jt 0). Its should-fix is applied too: the angle is refused while the hub is being pulled.   - Added to `elven.html` as the "Crescent cleats" join option, last in the join row (gap 34). GitDiscuss's stem carries three silver willow leaves hanging point down on arched stalks. **Near:** small crescents and leaves peep out of the stems, and the horn tips glimmer. **Ready:** the crescents, the seated leaves and a down arrow for the slide are traced in dashed moonlight. **Joining:** the casings close with GitDiscuss riding 26 px high (scaled in a shade), and the fittings unfurl. Then GitDiscuss slides straight down along Jarvis with silver streaks trailing up the leaves, until each leaf drops into a crescent's arms, its tip tucked behind the band. It settles 2.5 px past and back with a glint and a ring of moonlight. The horns curl shut over the leaves like tendrils, and the moonstones kindle top first as the join light (moonlight running, grey paused), with a glint down the seam. Pulling the casings apart does nothing. **Release:** draw the cleats up (the horns uncurl as you lift; let go early and they curl shut again), tap them, or press Enter. Everything plays back, with the streaks now trailing below, and GitDiscuss slides up off the crescents before the casings part. Its polish is applied too: dy is exactly 0 at the ends, the phase labels match the unfurl and furl timing, the streaks trail the motion on release, and the CLX constant is clearer. In a Drain shade the casing turns liquid and runs down into what holds it: unlike Sink or Stow, nothing moves whole, and unlike Cinch, nothing is gathered. A round iron flap on its face swings up on a hinge with a dart, opening a brass-collared outlet with a grille. The shell then drains. The full-height outline is drawn in its own layer under the parchment, fixed on the stage while the box shrinks. Its ink falls inside it under a wavering meniscus (pale highlight, dark underline) that dips toward the outlet. Above the level it leaves a pale stain, three tide lines and eight runs, each ending in a bead on the surface. A dashed whirl turns over the outlet as the trough's ink rises. The empty outline then washes away from the top down, its edge running in tongues, while the box draws down to the trough and the iron scroll and lantern are lowered. A ripple runs along the ink. Light comes up through the grille as the status, reflected on the ink (amber running, cold blue and dim paused, sea-green for Checks). The name is chalked on the trough's face left to right with chalk dust, and the value drops onto a pinned parchment slip beside it, clear of the outlet. Nothing moves at rest, unshading plays it back, and the strap stays at the joining end, so drained shells still join. Listed in the index and the page hint, and the "Plain draw-in" text lists it.   - Added to `combined.html` as the "Graving dock" mix, last in the picker. It uses the `stoneelf` pair: stone Jarvis with no corbel, and elven GitDiscuss (mirrored) and Checks. The gap is 30. At rest Jarvis stands on a stone footing under its buttress, with a sunk quatrefoil, and a horned gargoyle juts into the gap. **Near:** the slab's end slides out of the footing, and the gargoyle's jaw drops with a drop swelling on it. **Ready:** the slab, pier, keel blocks and waterline are traced in moonlight behind the widgets. **Joining:** a slab slides out of the footing, with a roll moulding, cusped blind arches and a leafy drain mask on its face. GitDiscuss is carried in 34 px above it. The far pier (gablet, pinnacle, pointed lamp niche) rises up through the slab, overshoots and settles, shedding grit. The gargoyle pours and the dock fills between the buttress and the pier. The leaf is set down on the water, rocks once and spreads rings. Then the mask's mouth opens. The water whirls and falls away, and the leaf sinks with it onto two keel blocks, whose lips come up just over its rim. It lands with a squash and a glint at each lip. The last of the water runs from the mask, and the moonstone in the niche kindles as the join light (warm while both run, cold blue if either is paused). Afloat, the water is drawn over the leaf's belly only, below local y 122, so it stays clear of the tabs and buttons. **Release:** tap or pull the slab or the pier, or press Enter. The lamp goes out, the gargoyle refills the dock, the leaf floats up off its blocks and is lifted clear, the dock drains, the pier sinks and GitDiscuss is carried out. A long enough pull refloats it under your hand. The dock follows the leaf's height when shaded. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, undefined helpers, name clashes or NaN, and confirmed continuity at both ends. Its must-fixes are applied. Afloat, the water reached the bottom of the buttons' rings, so the draft is now 3 px and the bob is smaller. During the pier's overshoot a gap showed under it, so the pier's base now runs below the slab's top, where it is clipped. Its should-fixes are applied too. The keel blocks and the gargoyle are now drawn behind the widgets, so a GitDiscuss carried in low, or dragged across the buttress, is never covered by them. Only the blocks' lips are drawn in front, once the leaf is over them. Most of its polish is applied as well: the jaw stays open through a join dropped at Ready, the drain stream no longer thins in one frame, the pinnacle is in the hit area, the hold text is corrected and dead code is removed. Left as is: the whirl sits over the submerged belly, which reads as water in front of it, and on a rejoin mid-release the bob and the squash can pop in. A tall silver bough grows from Jarvis's top knop, arches over GitDiscuss and lets a beaded silver chain down plumb to a ring through GitDiscuss's crest, so GitDiscuss hangs like a scale beam and turns about that ring (it rises past the 50° sweep of GitDiscuss's raised end, so no tilt meets it). GitDiscuss tips until the lantern hangs straight under the ring, so the lantern's place is the rail's depth times tanθ and the marks crowd toward the middle as on a real steelyard. **Near:** the knop buds and the crest glimmers. **Ready:** bough, chain, rail and the lantern's place traced in moonlight. **Joining:** the bough climbs with leaves opening behind its tip, the chain is let down and the ring closes on the crest with a glint, the drops grow and the rail runs out from the middle, the lantern is hung, then slides out swinging back on its ring while GitDiscuss tips after it, a little past and back; the lantern kindles (warm running, grey paused) and a glint runs down the chain. **Angle:** drag the lantern along the rail (it leads, GitDiscuss follows a moment behind and settles on the nearest mark), drag GitDiscuss's nameplate, the slider or ↑ ↓; each mark passed glints and swings the lantern, and a moonstone tag reads the mark. **Release:** tap the bough or chain (or Enter). Engine: optional angle hooks `kx` (knot place for a pivot away from the seam, via `knotX`), `top` (ornament above the pair in `pairBox`) and `poise` (grab/move/drop/cancel for dragging a weight), with `drag.poise` in the pointer handlers, `pairHeld` and the phase line; no other join sets them. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no must-fixes: no syntax errors, clashes, TDZ or NaN, and it confirmed the pivot agrees across the CSS origin, pairBox, forkKnot and the overlay, the bend's continuity, the clearance from Jarvis and the bough, and the stage fit. Its should-fixes are applied: a tap on the lantern while it was still settling, or a release mid-settle, left GitDiscuss between marks (both now settle on a mark), the lantern drag now counts as holding the pair, the pair's box takes in the bough and the lantern, and the follow loop's first frame no longer steps backwards. Two of my own top-level names (`BL`, `blK`) clashed with existing ones and were renamed before review. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **wire** · Angle: bead scale (Balance), the second item of the Balance round (elven's lantern counterpoise opened it; wire had waited longest, its last item being the Stretch round's memory wire), from the goldsmith's hand scale. Elven's is a steelyard, read by sliding one weight; this is an equal-arm balance read by counting, so the angle is set by how six pearls are shared between two pans
  - Added to `wire.html` as the "Bead scale" join option, last in the join row (gap 56, −45° to +45° in hard 15° steps, default +30°). GitDiscuss is the beam. A twisted brass gallows stands on Jarvis's roof on scroll feet, with a scrolled brace and a curled end, and lets a brass chain down plumb over GitDiscuss's middle. The chain ends in a lyre: a copper scale arc with seven gold seeds, its wires running down to a pin. GitDiscuss hangs on the pin by a brass bail on its crest and turns about it, and a fine tongue rising from the bail points at the seeds. Hammered copper pans hang plumb on three brass cords from eyes under its ends, and each pearl moved from one pan to the other tips it one 15° mark (far pan holds 3 + angle/15). **Near:** Jarvis's roof and GitDiscuss's crest warm. **Ready:** gallows, chain, lyre and level pans traced in lamplight. **Joining:** the post climbs hot out of the roof and the arm runs out with its scrolls, the bail rises from the crest, the chain is let down link by link, the lyre closes on the bail and the pin slides through with a glint, the eyes are drawn and the pans drop on their cords. The pearls are then counted in one at a time, alternately, and GitDiscuss rocks half a mark toward each as it lands, ending exactly at the set angle. The tongue's bead and the seed it points at kindle as the join light (amber running, labradorite if both are paused) and a glint runs down the chain. **Angle:** tap a pan (or focus it and press Enter) and a pearl lobs into it from the other pan, then GitDiscuss tips after it, swinging a little past and back (straight onto the stop at ±45°); or drag a pan's top pearl onto the other pan (dropped anywhere else, it hops home); or use GitDiscuss's nameplate, the slider or ↑ ↓, and the pearls hop across to match. A tag beside the gallows reads the angle and the pans. **Release:** tap the gallows or chain (or Enter); the timeline plays back, pearls lifted out first. Engine: optional angle hooks `span` (the pair's own vertical extent, in `pivotSpan`), `rock` (a settling swing in `tweenAngle`) and `go` (through a new `goAngle` used by the slider and arrow keys); the transform origin now handles a negative `x0` (a pivot inside GitDiscuss). No other join sets them. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no load-order, name or NaN problems, confirmed every other join is unchanged, and checked the clearances (GitDiscuss's swept corner passes 11 px from Jarvis, pans 20 px) and the stage fit. Its must-fixes are applied: dragging from an empty pan acted as a tap and moved a pearl, and a carried pearl counted as dropped on the other pan wherever it was let go (it now has to land within reach of it). Its should-fixes are applied too: a softer settling swing with no flat spot against the ±45° stop, room for the rock when joining at 0°, and hopping pearls kept below GitDiscuss's casing. So is its polish: pan hit areas moved off the casing, no pearl hops while one is carried, and the post's hit area stops short of Jarvis's roof. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **hollow** · Angle: rain ballast (Balance), the third item of the Balance round (elven's lantern counterpoise, wire's bead scale; hollow had waited longest, its last item being the Stretch round's herse). Elven's balance is read by where one weight is slid and wire's by counting pearls; this one is read by volume, from a city where it always rains, its rain chains, and the water-balance lifts that rise and fall by filling a tank
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Rain ballast" join option, last in the join row (gap 56, −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°). An iron crook (scrolled brace, curled finial, a rain hopper at its end) climbs out of Jarvis's strap spike and lets a rain chain down to a bail on GitDiscuss's crest, and GitDiscuss turns about the bail's pin. Two glass tanks with chalk marks are let down under its belly, piped through a hand pump (a rocking beam with bone knobs and red lashing, a brass collar, a drain cock). Rain runs down the chain from the hopper while the pump fills both tanks evenly. The far tank holds 3 + angle/15 of six gulps, and because the tanks turn with the shell, the rain lies level across the tilted glass (one clip path per tank). The pump's sight glass is the join light (amber, or cold blue-white if either is paused). **Angle:** tap a tank (or press ↑ ↓ on the focused tanks): the beam rocks, a surge runs along the pipe and the gulp arrives, then GitDiscuss follows a beat behind and the rain ripples. A dry tank gives nothing. You can also drag a tank or the nameplate to tip it by hand, or use the slider, and the water follows. **Release:** tap the crook or chain. The water evens out, the rain is let out of the drain cock, the tanks come off and the crook sinks. Engine: an optional `box` hook in `pairBox`, set only by this join, so Checks keeps clear of the crook and tanks. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax, name or NaN problems. It confirmed the water stays level, the clearances (about 12 px under the crook arm at the 47° extreme, and the swept end well clear of Jarvis) and the release timeline. Its must-fix is applied: a press on the crook that wandered off used to separate the widgets, and now only a tap releases. So are its should-fixes. The slider taking over mid-gulp no longer leaves the water stranded or gets overridden. A press elsewhere mid-gulp lets the gulp finish. The box only counts the crook while it stands, and the chain is drawn up before the hopper goes.
