- [x] **gothic** · Angle: counterweight (Balance), the fourth item of the Balance round (elven's lantern counterpoise, wire's bead scale, hollow's rain ballast; gothic had waited longer than felt). Elven reads a slid weight, wire a count of pearls and hollow a volume of water; here GitDiscuss is weighed against a stone counterweight over a wheel, and the angle is read from the weight's height, as medieval churches hung font covers and lamps against counterweights so one hand could set them anywhere
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Counterweight" join option, last in the join row (gap 64, square ends, −45° to +45° in 15° steps, default +30°). GitDiscuss turns about a wrought-iron wheel in the gap (six spokes with C-scrolls, a grooved rim, a gilt quatrefoil hub), keyed to it by two iron straps nailed to its end with split curls; the axle sits on an iron bracket from Jarvis's end pier. A crocketed limestone counterweight (moulded cap, band of gilt flowers, gilt knop) hangs on an iron bail from a laid rope wound in the rim's groove and dropping from the wheel's left side. The rope winds on as the wheel turns, so the weight rises exactly as far as the groove turns (rr per radian): GitDiscuss sinks as the weight goes up, and an iron tongue on the bail reads a gauge of seven marks cut into Jarvis's end pier, the level mark gilded and the mark in use lit. **Near:** the stone warms where the bracket plate, gauge and straps will go. **Ready:** wheel, rope, weight at its mark, gauge and GitDiscuss's swing are chalked out. **Joining:** the bracket runs out with grit, the gauge is cut, the straps run out, the wheel drops spinning onto the axle and lands with a clank and a spark, the rope is paid out, the weight is swung in onto the hook and bounces, then GitDiscuss swings until the weight balances it (a little past and back, the weight swaying, a click and glint at each mark), and the hub's glass kindles (amber while both run, cold blue if either is paused) with a glint down the rope. **Angle:** drag the weight (pixel for pixel), turn the wheel, swing GitDiscuss's nameplate, use the slider or ↑ ↓ on the focused weight; it settles on the nearest mark, and a parchment label reads the angle and mark. **Release:** tap the gilt hub (or Enter): hub dark, swing level, weight unhooked, rope wound in, wheel lifted off, bracket and straps drawn in. Listed in the index and the page hint. The reviewer (reading only) found no must-fixes: no syntax or name errors, correct rope winding direction, the pivot matching the engine's transform, continuous poses both ways, correct hit routing and stage range, and GitDiscuss clearing Jarvis by about 3 px at the full-height overshoot. Its should-fixes are applied. The wheel is smaller (R 29) and the weight hangs lower, so during the +45° overshoot the hook stays below the rope's tangent and the bail stays off the rim. The rope arc's large-arc flag now follows the span. Its polish is applied too: the hook, drag and gauge all move at the groove radius, and the wheel's hit circle no longer reaches the facades' ends. Left as is: shaded, the gauge shows only the marks that fit on the strip (the level mark falls below it), and reversing mid-swing jumps the pose, as on the other angled joins. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.

Lift balances spring at angle = 60° − 15° per puff: one puff +45°, four level, seven −45°, so the envelope's size is the reading. **Near:** the knuckles peek out of Jarvis's end. **Ready:** dashed ghosts of knuckles, spring and balloon at the set angle. **Joining (12 fps):** the cushions close, the knuckles pop out with fibres, the spring is hooked across, the basket pops up off the roof loop and rises on its tether while the burner roars and the envelope fills from a limp sack, then GitDiscuss swings to balance, a little past and back; the pilot light kindles as the join light (gold, cold blue if paused) and a glint runs up the tether. **Angle:** tap the balloon (or Enter / ↑ on it) to fire the burner: the envelope swells first and GitDiscuss rises after it with a rock; tap the tassel (Enter / ↓) to vent a puff (wool wisps curl out of the crown) and it sinks; full or at one puff, the burner or vent still goes and nothing moves. Engine: an optional `boxTop` hook in `pairBox` (Checks keeps clear of the balloon), and render() keeps focus on the overlay's burner and vent buttons across redraws. Listed in the index and the page hint. The flame is the join light (amber, or cold blue-white if either widget is paused). Wax pools in the pan and runs off its spout in a thin stream down between the straps, led by three tongues, with runs bulging over the strap edges, until it fills the gap. It sets from hot amber to chalk white with puffs of steam, and a last bead hangs off the bottom. **Release:** draw the seam down, tap it, or press Enter. The flame flares, the wax softens, slumps and runs off into the fog in drops, the flame is snuffed with a curl of smoke and the arm swings home. A rejoin part-way through a release blends the two poses, so nothing jumps. Listed in the index and the page hint. Wax sets, water freezes and resin cures; here the seam is bronze poured into a loam mould that is broken off it, from medieval founders who cast bells and fonts on site and masons who ran metal into dovetail sockets to key stones together
  - Added to `gothic.html` as the "Cast bronze" join option, last in the join row (gap 30, square ends), with an index entry and a page hint. **Near:** dovetail sockets are cut into both facing ends with a glint. **Ready:** the seam, its crest and the pouring cup are chalked across the gap. **Joining:** the facades close and a loam mould banded in iron, with a pouring cup and two risers, is let down into the gap and lands in grit. A clay crucible on a long iron shank comes in over the cup, tips about its lip and pours a white-hot stream. Heat glows through the loam as it fills, the sockets beyond the mould's edges fill with hot metal as the level passes them, then the risers glow and smoke. The crucible rights itself and leaves, the mould cracks and falls off in three pieces (each clipped between jagged cracks, turning as it drops in dust), and the seam stands orange in the gap. It is a bronze strip with a gabled, crocketed crest and fleur finial, a sunk panel with a running stem and leaves, quatrefoils (three, or one when shaded) and a dovetail key in each socket. It cools through red to bronze, the runner cone is knocked off the finial with a spark, and the glass boss in the middle quatrefoil kindles as the join light (amber while both run, cold blue if either is paused). **Release:** tap the bronze or press Enter. The reviewer (reading only) found no must-fixes: it found balanced code, escaped strings, no TDZ or NaN, the same joined pose in both directions, the stream meeting the cup, and the dovetails inside the end piers. The suds pop, steam rises as the felt dries paler, and the nep, now a hard felt ball, is the join light (gold while both run, cold blue if either is paused), with a glint. **Release:** tap the band (or Enter). The ball dims, a hand carder (walnut paddle, wire teeth, teal felt grip) combs down the seam in three strokes, each teasing the felt back toward roving, then the tufts drift off before the cushions part and the end tufts fade back in. The reviewer (reading only) found no must-fixes. Its should-fix is applied: the end tufts popped back in at the end of a release, in the fluffed pose. So are most of its polish items: the tufts jumped at the start of a join from the button, the colour tufts darkened all at once instead of under the waterline, one phase label came early, the join ball was small, and the drifting roving could leave the top of the stage. Left as is: with the pair at the top of the stage, the kettle's steam wisps just touch the top edge. I found and fixed one syntax error on reading the code back: an invalid conditional assignment target in the tuft drawing. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run.
- [x] **combined** · Half-coupler for the Pour kind: bronze first, then ice (Model A, after the Pour round). Every listed item was built and the Pour round had ended with felt's wet felt, so a combined item was next; the combined models run C, A, D, E, B and the Balance round's C means A comes next. Gothic and elven had met at an authored seam (the vine up the tracery) and in a container (the graving dock), never at a standard face. Every Pour join filled its gap with its own skin's material; this asks what a standard face is when nobody owns the whole gap. The answer comes from two-shot casting: the face is a parting plate, and the hotter pour goes first
  - Added to `combined.html` as the "Bronze first, then ice" mix, last in the picker (pair `stoneelf`: gothic Jarvis, elven GitDiscuss and Checks; gap 44; 4.8 s joining, 3 s releasing), with an index entry and a page hint. The face is a blued steel plate with a gauge stamp and a dovetail tooth every 18 px (7 deep, 6 at the root, 10 at the tip). It fixes the tooth, not the count, so the full seam has three teeth and a shaded one has one. **Near:** a dovetail socket is cut into Jarvis's buttress with a glint, and rime creeps along GitDiscuss's stem. **Ready:** the bronze half, the ice half and the plate are traced. **Joining:** the casings close and the plate drops into the gap with a ring. A loam mould banded in iron is let down against Jarvis's end and lands with grit. A crucible on an iron shank swings in, tips and pours a white-hot stream: the heat shows through the loam, the socket and the plate's tooth pockets fill with hot metal as the level passes them, and the riser fills and smokes. The crucible leaves, the mould cracks off in two halves, and the bronze (gabled crest, finial, sunk quatrefoil, a key in the socket) stands orange and cools. The plate is drawn straight up and steams. A silver rim is traced out from the stem both ways round a leaf-pointed mould. A silver ewer drifts down and pours moonwater against the bronze, which steams and quenches dark along its face, and frost ferns grow in as the water freezes round the teeth. The parting line (a cold shut: the pours never fuse) kindles as the join light, gold or cold blue if either widget is paused, with a glint running down it. **Release:** tap the seam (or Enter). The line goes dark, the ice thaws and drains out of its lower point, the rim fades, the bronze key is lifted out of its socket toward you, the socket closes and the casings part. The reviewer (reading only) found no must-fixes: the code parses, there are no name or id clashes, and the state matches at jt 1 in both directions. Its should-fixes are applied. The drain stream popped in at full width (it now ramps). The room above the pair was too small for the crucible (now 70, and the plate drops from lower). The plate appeared out of nowhere (it now fades in). So is most of its polish: the socket now eases in on a button join, the tooth pockets glow while pouring, the ice now reaches round the stem, the ready trace now draws the plate, the crucible no longer touches the cup, and the ewer's stream waits for the ewer to arrive. Left as is: rejoining partway through a release jumps back to the casting stage (as in other mixes), and the tooth count changes in hard steps while shading a joined pair. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **felt** · Shade: iron (Press), a new shade kind opening a Press round. Every listed item was built and the Pour round had ended with combined, so a shade round was next (rounds run morph, angle, join, shade). Felt had opened the fewest rounds (three, like gothic and wire) and had waited longest of those, its last opener being the Wedge round. In a Press shade the cushion is pressed flat: not folded, rolled, drained or put away.
  - Added to `felt.html` as the "Iron" shade option, last in the shade row (58 px strip, 48 for Checks; 4.6 s; foot-anchored), with an index entry, a page hint and a mention in the "Plain deflate" text. A raspberry felt toy iron (cream handle, silver soleplate, a dial and a pilot light) lands on the cushion's left end and dents it. Then it glides along and back in three passes at 12 fps, each taking a third of the puff out. The cushion's top edge follows the sole, so the stitching and name band flatten with it. A small bow wave rises ahead of the toe, the pressed felt takes a sheen and a crisp edge, and felt steam puffs at every press and turn and trails from the heel. The iron lifts and turns in the air while a cream iron-on name tape drops onto the flat felt under a glossy transfer film. One last pass leaves the name (satin stitch) and value (cross-stitch) on the tape behind the heel. Then the iron lifts away and the last of the film is wiped off. **The pilot light, and a little felt iron sewn at the tape's end, are the status colour.** The box height follows the felt's highest point, so joins and neighbours see the strip shrink pass by pass. Unshading peels the tape off, and a burst of steam puffs the cushion back up with a bounce. Nothing moves at rest. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, undefined names, NaN or clashing ids, and text that fits at both widths. Its must-fixes are applied: the height no longer drops 72 px in one frame at the end, the film over the tape's left end is wiped instead of vanishing, and the outline, band and shadow are traced exactly like the casing's, so the stitch dashes don't shift when the drawing hands over. Its polish is applied too: tilt and bow wave ease in and out of each glide, the landing overshoot dents the felt instead of sinking the iron, the sheen fades as the cushion rises instead of flickering in the bounce, and the unshade steam only runs when unshading. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
- [x] **hollow** · Shade: map press (Press), the second item of the Press round (felt's iron opened it; hollow had waited longest, its last item being the Pour round's candle wax), from the cartographer's screw press that printed maps from cut blocks
  - Added to `hollow.html` as the "Map press" shade option, last in the shade row (4.2 s in, 2.4 s out; foot-anchored like the fog sink and ink drain), with an index entry, a page hint and a mention in the "Plain draw-in" text. The parchment, tabs and studs fade, an iron bed slides up under the shell's foot, and an iron press drops onto it with a jolt and a puff of dust at each foot. The press has a crosshead with curled horns, posts standing behind the shell clear of the scroll, a brass-ringed nut, a threaded screw, a bar with bone knobs, and a riveted platen. A blank parchment slip slides onto the shell's back from the outer end. The bar spins and the platen screws down onto the arch. Three hard pulls follow, each half a turn of the bar. With each one the shell is a third flatter: its own outline is redrawn with the arch's rise as a parameter (`pressD`), so the arch visibly flattens under the platen. Pale cracks flash down the chitin below the slip, ink beads are squeezed out between the belly plates onto the bed, and dust puffs off both ends. The box steps down pull by pull (`cinch = pressP`). The bar spins back and the platen lifts off a printed slip: the name and value in black and red, letterpress-bitten and glossy with wet ink that dries. Then the press is lifted off and the bed sinks away. **A glass bead by the strap kindles as the status** (amber running, cold blue paused, sea-green for Checks). Nothing moves at rest, and unshading plays it back, so the shell swells up under the platen. The scroll, lantern and strap stay, so pressed shells still join. The reviewer (reading only) found no syntax errors, undefined names, TDZ problems, duplicate identifiers or NaN, with no must-fixes. It confirmed that the platen meets the arch, the box steps 130→44 and 78→36, the slip text fits at both widths, and the mirroring for GitDiscuss is right. I applied its polish: the casing's faint highlight lines are kept so nothing vanishes on the first frame, the slip and platen sit 1.5 px lower so the slip's corners stay inside the flattened outline, ink beads land where they rest, the bed now reaches the shell's outer lobe, and the left post clears the scroll. I also moved the cracks below the slip, where they had been hidden. Not checked in a browser or parser: Playwright and node needed approval this run, so the code was checked by reading only.
